- Passages 3:161 and 3:162 (volume 7, pp. 356-366, reports 8155-8171): 3:161 is completed with the threat against the one who defrauds spoil or charity, the hadith reports transmitted from Abu Hurayra, Ibn 'Abbas, Abu Humayd al-Sa'idi, 'Abdallah ibn Unays, Ibn 'Umar (Sa'd ibn 'Ubada) and 'Ubayd ibn Abi 'Ubayd, with the editor's long notes on narrators, corrections to the printed text and manuscript and references, Qatada's report of the herald's cry, and the full repayment of every soul (8168); 3:162 gives the two views on the comparison of the one who follows God's pleasure with the one who incurs His anger (al-Dahhak and Ibn Ishaq) and al-Tabari's choice. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict; the commentary continues with 3:163 on p. 367.
- Passages 3:160 and 3:161 (volume 7, pp. 347-355, reports 8135-8154): 3:160 gives al-Tabari on divine help and forsaking and the call to rely on God, with Ibn Ishaq's report 8135 and the editor's notes on the text. 3:161 is begun with the disagreement of the readers on the verb of ghulul (the reading with fatha on the ya, the reading with damma on the ya), the reports of the lost red cloak at Badr with the editor's notes on the narrators Khusayf, Qaz'a and others, the views on the share of the scouts, on concealing revelation and on being betrayed by companions or accused of betrayal, the lexical notes, and al-Tabari's choice with his reply to an objection from the other reading. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict; the commentary on verse 161 continues on p. 356.
- Passages 3:156 to 3:159 (volume 7, pp. 336-346, reports 8116-8134): 3:156 is completed (God as the one who hastens or delays death, the believers' encouragement to fight, and Ibn Ishaq's report 8116 with the editor's note on a possible omission by the copyist); 3:157 gives death or killing in God's way as better than worldly gathering, the omitted answer to the condition with a Basran grammarian's explanation and the editor's emendations; 3:158 gives the return to God and al-Tabari's account of the entering lam; 3:159 is begun with the extra 'ma', the meaning of gentleness and harshness (reports 8119-8124, including the description of the Prophet in the Torah as transmitted by Qatada), the command to forgive and ask forgiveness (8125), the three views on why he was told to consult (8126-8131), al-Tabari's choice, and resolve and reliance (8132-8134); the commentary on verse 159 continues on p. 347. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passage 3:155 (volume 7, pp. 327-330, reports 8098-8106) is completed: the rest of 'Umar's account with the editor's notes on its narrators, the views that the verse means all who fled, only those who reached Medina, or named men, the accounts of 'Uthman ibn 'Affan with 'Uqba and Sa'd ibn 'Uthman at al-Jal'ab, and the meaning of the pardon. Passage 3:156 (pp. 330-335, reports 8107-8115) opens with the warning against resembling the deniers who say of those lost on a journey or in fighting that they would have lived had they stayed, the identification with 'Abdallah ibn Ubayy or all the hypocrites, the two meanings of travelling in the land, 'ghuzza' with the editor's notes on the line of Ru'ba, the grammar of the relative 'alladhina' with a past verb in a future sense, and the sorrow in their hearts; the commentary on this verse continues on p. 336. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passage 3:154 (volume 7, pp. 319-326, reports 8083-8097) is completed: the last reports on the drowsiness, the second group and the thought of the Jahiliyya, the two ways of parsing 'a group', the hypocrites' saying with 'Abdallah ibn Ubayy and Mu'attib ibn Qushayr, the two readings of 'all', the testing and sifting of what is in the breasts, and Ibn Ishaq's and al-Hasan's explanations with the editor's chain notes. Passage 3:155 (pp. 326-327, report 8098 begun) opens with the wording and meaning of the verse on those who turned away at Uhud and the first view on who is meant, with 'Umar's sermon account, which continues on p. 328. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passage 3:153 (volume 7, pp. 311-315, reports 8067-8071) is completed: the last reports on the two griefs including Mujahid's and 'Ubayd ibn 'Umayr's accounts of Abu Sufyan at the glen, al-Tabari's choice of the view with his argument, the editor's notes on the manuscript and the word 'Ibn Abi Kabsha', and the closing clause on God's knowledge of what they do. Passage 3:154 (pp. 315-318, reports 8072-8082 begun) opens with the security and the drowsiness, the two readings of the verb, and the reports of Abu Talha, Anas, 'Abd al-Rahman ibn 'Awf, Qatada and al-Rabi' on the drowsiness at Uhud and the hypocrites who stayed awake; the commentary on this verse continues on p. 319. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passage 3:153 (volume 7, pp. 303-310, reports 8054-8066): the call of the Messenger to the men from behind, the sense of being repaid with distress and of the word for reward, the preposition and its analogues, the three views of the two griefs, and the long reports of al-Suddi and Ibn Ishaq on the glen, the wounds of the Messenger, the death of Mus'ab ibn 'Umayr, Ka'b ibn Malik's recognition of him and the exchange with Abu Sufyan, with the editor's notes on poetry, words and sources. The commentary on this verse continues on p. 311. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passage 3:152 (volume 7, pp. 295-299, reports 8033-8046) is completed: the last reports on those wanting this world and those wanting the next with Ibn Mas'ud's remark, the diverting of the believers and the testing, the pardon and the favour, with al-Hasan's account of the losses at Uhud and the editor's manuscript notes. Passage 3:153 (pp. 299-302, reports 8047-8053) opens with the two readings of the first verb, the argument of those who read it as flight along the valley and of those who read it as climbing the mountain, and al-Tabari's choice; the commentary continues on p. 303. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passage 3:152 (volume 7, pp. 287-294, reports 8012-8032): the sense of the word for cutting down and of 'by His leave', the loss of heart, dispute and disobedience of the archers at Uhud with the reports of Qatada, Ibn 'Abbas, al-Rabi', Ibn Ishaq and others, al-Tabari's mention of another view on the order of the words with the editor's notes on its sources, and the opening of the clause on those who want this world and those who want the next. The commentary on this verse continues on p. 295. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:151-152 (volume 7, pp. 279-286, reports 8002-8011): 3:151 on the dread cast into the hearts of the deniers (reports of Ibn Ishaq and al-Suddi, with the editor's note on a break in the old division of his manuscript), and the first part of 3:152 on God's promise at Uhud, with the reports of al-Suddi, al-Bara', Ibn 'Abbas, Ibn Ishaq's composite account of the battle and al-Zubayr, and the editor's corrections of the text. The commentary on this verse continues on p. 287. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:146-150 (volume 7, pp. 271-278, reports 7983-8001): the close of 3:146, then 3:147 (the saying of the ribbiyyun, the word for excess and its readings, firm feet, al-Tabari's reading of the verse as a reproach for Uhud and his reasons for the accusative reading with the editor's grammar notes), 3:148 (the reward of this world and the next), 3:149 (the prohibition on obeying the deniers) and 3:150 (God as the Master), as transmitted, without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:145-146 (volume 7, pp. 263-270, reports 7955-7983): the close of 3:145 (the reward of the thankful) and 3:146 up to report 7983 (the two readings of the first word and of the verb, al-Tabari's choice of the passive with his reasons, the grammar and meaning of the word for the groups with the prophets, the reports of thousands, groups, scholars and followers, the editor's note on an unreadable place-name, and the last part of the verse on not weakening), which continues on p. 271. Also rewords a verse-like fragment in 3:145. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:144-145 (volume 7, pp. 255-262, reports 7943-7955): the close of 3:144 (the end of al-Suddi's account of Uhud, the report that the Messenger was killed and the replies of Anas ibn al-Nadr and the Ansari, the occasion reports, and al-Tabari's grammar of a question placed on a condition) and 3:145 up to report 7955 (the written term, three views on the accusative of the verbal noun with al-Tabari's choice, and desiring the reward of this world or the next), which continues on p. 263. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:142-144 (volume 7, pp. 247-254, reports 7929-7943): the close of 3:142 (the accusative by diversion, al-Hasan's reading); 3:143 complete (those who wished for a day like Badr, the editor's notes on Ibn Ishaq's report and on the Sira variant); and 3:144 up to report 7943 (the messenger who dies or is killed, the thankful, Abu Bakr as trustee of the thankful in the reports from Ali and al-Ala' ibn Badr with the editor's note on Sayf ibn Umar, the occasion, and the opening of al-Suddi's account of the archers at Uhud), which continues on p. 255. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:140-142 (volume 7, pp. 237-246, reports 7893-7928): the close of 3:140 (the editor's note on the two readings of the word for a wound, al-Tabari's choice of the fatha reading, the alternation of the days of Badr and Uhud with the Abu Sufyan exchange on the mountain, the grammar of God's knowing the believers, taking martyrs, and the wrongdoers); 3:141 complete (purifying and effacing); and the opening of 3:142, which continues on p. 247. Also reworded the English line of 3:133 that had typed verse wording. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:137-140 (volume 7, pp. 228-236, reports 7867-7892): 3:137 (the earlier patterns of punishment, travelling to see the end of the deniers, the meaning of sunna with the editor's notes on the poets Labid and Sulayman ibn Qatta); 3:138 (what 'this' points to, al-Tabari's choice, guidance and admonition); 3:139 (consolation after Uhud, the ravine, Khalid ibn al-Walid's cavalry on the mountain and the archers); and the opening of 3:140 (the two readings of the word for a wound), which continues on p. 237. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:135-136 (volume 7, pp. 218-227, reports 7846-7866): the close of 3:135 (the indecency taken as adultery and wronging oneself, the report that the verse lightened for this community what was written on the doors of the Children of Israel, Iblis weeping, the ablution-and-prayer report of Abu Bakr through Ali with the editor's long tracing and gradings, the grammar of the exception, and al-Tabari's choice among three views of persisting and of knowing, with the editor's note on report 7863); and 3:136 complete (the reward of forgiveness and gardens). Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:133-135 (volume 7, pp. 209-217, reports 7831-7845): the close of 3:133 (the question where the Fire is if Paradise is as wide as the heavens and earth, answered with the night and the day in reports from the Prophet, Umar and Ibn Abbas, with the editor's long notes on the chain through al-Tanukhi and Sa'id ibn Abi Rashid and the Abu Hurayra parallel); 3:134 (spending in ease and hardship, swallowing anger, pardon and doing good, with the editor's note on report 7842); and the opening of 3:135, which continues on p. 218. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:128-133 (volume 7, pp. 201-208, reports 7820-7830): the close of 3:128 (the dawn prayer for the weak believers and the cursing of named tribes, reports 7820-7821, with the editor's long tracing and his quotation of al-Hafiz Ibn Hajar on when the verse came down); 3:129 (God's sovereignty, forgiveness and mercy); 3:130 (the pre-Islamic doubling of debts, the Thaqif and Banu al-Mughira, the camel-age scheme in report 7826 with the editor's glosses); 3:131 (the Fire prepared for the unbelievers); 3:132 (obedience, and the view that it rebukes those who left their posts at Uhud); and the start of 3:133 (the breadth of Paradise, the poetic idiom of the omitted noun), which continues on p. 209. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:127-128 (volume 7, pp. 193-200, reports 7800-7819): the close of 3:127 (the view that it concerned those killed at Uhud, the sense of shaming and turning back) and 3:128 from its start: al-Tabari's reading of the verse and preference, Ibn Ishaq's report, the reports that it came down when the Prophet was wounded at Uhud (reports 7805-7817, with the editor's tracing through the Musnad, al-Tirmidhi, Muslim and others) and the reports that it came down when he prayed against certain men (7818-7819), which continue on p. 201. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:124-127 (volume 7, pp. 185-192, reports 7776-7799): the close of 3:124-125 with al-Tabari's choice of the kasra reading of the participle for marking and the reports on the angels' signs (wool, dyed wool, yellow turbans, piebald horses) with the editor's gradings and the other reading's argument; 3:126 (glad tidings and reassurance, victory from God alone, the editor's praise of al-Tabari's attention to divine oneness, the two divine names); and the start of 3:127 (cutting off a party of the unbelievers at Badr), which continues on p. 193. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:124-125 (volume 7, pp. 177-184, reports 7752-7775): the close of Abu Rafi's account of the news reaching Mecca, the capture of al-Abbas and the angel (report 7753), the reports on the number of angels (7754-7757), the view that the promise was conditional and met at the Confederates (report 7758 with the editor's grading of its chain), the views that they were not reinforced at Uhud (7759-7762), al-Tabari's preference with the editor's notes, the two views on the word for 'at once' (direction or anger, reports 7763-7775 and their consequences for Uhud), and the start of the readings of the participle for marking, which continues on p. 185. Given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:122, 3:123, 3:124 and 3:125 (volume 7, pp. 169-176, reports 7732-7752): the close of 3:122 (Ibn Ishaq's report 7732 and the plural reading ascribed to Ibn Mas'ud), 3:123 (the meaning of the help at Badr and of being few, the two accounts of why Badr is so named in reports 7734-7737, the numbers of the Badr fighters in reports 7738-7741 and the bidding to godwariness, report 7742) and 3:124-125 (commented together: the views that the angels were promised but not sent because Kurz ibn Jabir turned back, reports 7743-7746, and that they were sent, reports 7747-7752 with the editor's notes, the last of them, Abu Rafi's account of the news reaching Mecca, continuing on p. 177), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:121 and 3:122 (volume 7, pp. 161-168, reports 7714-7732): the close of 3:121 (the Confederates view, al-Tabari's preference for Uhud and his two answers on the Friday departure and the placing of the believers, reports 7715-7718 on the consultation and the armour, the verb's usage and a reading ascribed to Ibn Mas'ud, and God's hearing and knowledge, report 7719; the number of report 7714 is unreadable in the OCR and inferred from the sequence) and the opening of 3:122 (the two parties and the reports 7720-7732 on who they were, the meaning of the word for losing heart, and al-Tabari's account that their resolve was fear and not doubt or hypocrisy), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:119, 3:120 and 3:121 (volume 7, pp. 153-160, reports 7702-7713): the close of 3:119 (the plural of the fingertip word and the editor's notes on it, reports 7702-7704, the editor's long note on the chain of report 7701 with his summary of the Ibadiyya as he gives it, the command to perish in rage read as al-Tabari's supplication-form, and God's knowledge of what hearts hold), 3:120 (good touching and harm touching the believers, reports 7705-7707, patience and godwariness, the readings of the harm verb and the two grammatical explanations of the damma, the editor's note on the verses of Sawwar ibn al-Mudarrib quoted as evidence, and God encompassing what they do) and the opening of 3:121 (its link to the preceding verses, the view that the day is Uhud with reports 7708-7713, and the other view that it is the day of the Confederates, which begins on p. 160 and continues on p. 161), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:118 and 3:119 (volume 7, pp. 145-152, reports 7691-7701): the rest of 3:118 (what the open hatred of the tongues means, al-Tabari's rejection of the view that only hypocrites are meant, his argument that the People of the Book who held a covenant are those addressed, the masculine verb in Ibn Mas'ud's reading and the parallel verbs, the editor's notes on manuscript readings and on the 'to know truly' verb) and 3:119 (the demonstrative construction and the Kufan term for bringing near with the editor's notes quoting al-Suyuti, the collective sense of the word for the Book, the reports of Ibn Abbas, Qatada, Ibn Jurayj and Mujahid, the biting of fingertips, and the report of Abu al-Jawza' naming the Ibadiyya, with the editor's note on a narrator that continues on p. 153), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:117 and 3:118 (volume 7, pp. 137-144, reports 7676-7690): the close of 3:117 (the last reports on the bitter cold, including Ibn Zayd on the Arabic word for frost, and al-Tabari on why the loss of reward is no wrong done to them), and the opening of 3:118 (the intimate circle and its metaphor, the root of the word for being unable, the editor's disagreement with al-Tabari on it, the root of the word for ruin with the transmitted saying on blood and wounds, the occasion about Muslims and their Jewish allies, the reports naming the hypocrites, the saying on engraving and seeking the counsel of polytheists with the editor's long note on two narrators of one name, and the two readings of the word for hardship with al-Tabari's grammatical argument), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:113 to 3:117 (volume 7, pp. 128-136, reports 7662-7675): the close of 3:113 (the Ibn Mas'ud report on the late evening prayer with the editor's notes on its transmitters and its sound parallel, the report on the time between sunset and night prayers, al-Tabari's choice and his answer on the word for prostration), 3:114 (belief, commanding and forbidding, hastening to good), 3:115 (the variant readings in the third and second person and al-Tabari's choice, the root sense of denial of reward), 3:116 (the failure of wealth and children, the meaning of companions of the fire) and the opening of 3:117 (the parable of the cold wind and the tilth, with the reports on the word for bitter cold), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passage 3:113 (volume 7, pp. 119-127, reports 7644-7661): the praise of the believing group among the People of the Book (the grammarians' misreading of the word for alike and al-Tabari's answer, the reports of Ibn Abbas, Qatada and Ibn Jurayj on the Jews who accepted Islam, the second view with Ibn Mas'ud and al-Suddi, the meanings of the upright community with the report of al-Nu'man ibn Bashir and the editor's source notes, and the meanings of the hours of the night, including the report of Ibn Mas'ud on the late evening prayer), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:111 to 3:113 (volume 7, pp. 110-118, reports 7630-7643): the close of 3:111 (the promise of help and the raised verb), 3:112 (abasement and the covenant: the reports of al-Hasan, Mujahid, Qatada, Ikrima, al-Suddi, al-Rabi, Ibn Abbas, Ata, Ibn Zayd and al-Dahhak, the three grammatical views on the connective and the exception with the editor's notes on the lines cited, the wrath, humiliation and killing of prophets, and the lesson drawn by Qatada) and the opening of 3:113 (the two unequal groups of the People of the Book), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:110 and 3:111 (volume 7, pp. 101-109, reports 7606-7629): the long commentary on the praised community of 3:110 (the views that it means the emigrants, that it is the Companions, that it is conditional on commanding good, forbidding wrong and belief, that it means most responsive to Islam, and that it is the community in general, with the reports of Ibn Abbas, Umar, Mujahid, Abu Hurayra, al-Rabi, al-Hasan and Qatada, al-Tabari's choice of al-Hasan's view with the report on the seventy communities and the editor's source notes, and his answer on the past tense of the verb), the second half of 3:110 on the People of the Book, and 3:111 (the harm that is only hurt, and turning their backs), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:104 to 3:110 (volume 7, pp. 92-100, reports 7596-7605): the close of 3:104 (variant readings attributed to Ibn al-Zubayr and al-Dahhak's report on the called community), 3:105 (the warning against division, reports of al-Rabi, Ibn Abbas and al-Hasan), 3:106-107 (the day faces whiten and darken: the four views on who is addressed, from Qatada, al-Suddi, Abu Umama, Ubayy ibn Ka'b and al-Hasan, with al-Tabari's own choice and the editor's notes on the report of the Basin), 3:108 and 3:109 (God's justice, the grammarians' dispute over the repeated divine name, with the editor's notes on the line cited) and the opening of 3:110, given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:103 and 3:104 (volume 7, pp. 84-91, reports 7590-7595): the close of 3:103 (the origin of the Aws and Khazraj enmity in the killing of al-Hurr ibn Sumayr, the reports of Qatada and Ibn Mas'ud on brotherhood, al-Tabari on the edge of the pit and the pronoun that returns to the pit with the editor's notes on the lines cited, the reports of Qatada, al-Rabi, al-Suddi and al-Hasan ibn Hayy, and the closing clause on guidance, with the editor's note on the end of the fifth part of the manuscript and its colophon) and the opening of 3:104 (the community that calls to good, al-Tabari's glosses, and the report on a variant reading attributed to Uthman with the editor's note on its transmitters), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passage 3:103 (volume 7, pp. 76-83, reports 7581-7589): the close of Ibn Mas'ud's report on obedience and community (7581, with the editor's note on Isma'il ibn Hafs al-Ubulli), then al-Tabari on the reminder of God's favour, the Basran and Kufan grammarians on whether the clause on enmity is cut off from it and his choice, the reports of Qatada and al-Rabi (7582-7583), the 120-year war of the Aws and Khazraj (7584), and the reports behind it: Suwayd ibn al-Samit (7585), Abu al-Haysar Anas ibn Rafi' and Iyas ibn Mu'adh and the first Ansar at al-Aqaba with the editor's notes (7586), Ikrima (7587, 7589 with the editor's note on the added episode), al-Suddi on the war of Ibn Sumayr (7588), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:102 and 3:103 (volume 7, pp. 68-75, reports 7557-7580): the reports that the verse on due piety was abrogated by the verse of Surat al-Taghabun (7557-7560) with the editor's note on al-Tabari's silence and al-Nahhas's argument, Tawus on dying in Islam (7561), then the opening of 3:103 on holding fast to the rope of God: al-Tabari's gloss and the editor's note on the poetic line, the view that it is the community (7562-7563), the Qur'an and covenant (7564-7572, with the editor's tracing of the Abu Sa'id al-Khudri hadith), sincere monotheism (7573-7574), and the opening of the comment on the prohibition of division (7575-7580, with the editor's gradings of the seventy-two sects report), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:101 and 3:102 (volume 7, pp. 61-68, reports 7533-7556): al-Tabari on the question of how they could disbelieve with the signs and the Messenger among them (7533-7534), the meaning of holding fast with the editor's notes on the verse of poetry and the usage of the verb, the reported occasion of the Aws and Khazraj (7535) with the editor's gradings of its transmitters, and the opening of 3:102 with the reports on due piety from Ibn Mas'ud and others (7536-7551), Ibn Abbas's reading as striving (7552-7553), Tawus (7554-7555), and the view that the verse was abrogated (7556), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:99 and 3:100 (volume 7, pp. 53-60, reports 7524-7532): al-Tabari on the address to the People of the Book about turning people from the path (the pronoun, the word for crookedness, the meaning of being witnesses), the report of Shas ibn Qays and the day of Bu'ath with the editor's notes (7524), the view that the Jews and Christians who hid the description of the Prophet are meant (7525-7528), and the opening of 3:100 with the reports on Tha'laba ibn 'Unma (7529) and Mujahid (7530) and the warning of Qatada (7531-7532), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:97 and 3:98 (volume 7, pp. 45-52, reports 7498-7523): the close of Ibn Zayd's report on ability, al-Tabari's choice of ability to reach the sanctuary with his argument and his remark on the chains of the provisions-and-mount reports, the two readings of the word for pilgrimage (report 7499), the grammar of the relative clause, then the views on who disbelieves (those who deny the obligation, who hold no hope of reward or fear of sin, disbelief in God and the last day, in the signs, in the House, or leaving it until death, reports 7500-7521, with the editor's notes on Nufay' ibn al-Harith) and al-Tabari's choice, and the opening of the commentary on 3:98 (7522-7523), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passage 3:97 (volume 7, pp. 37-44, reports 7474-7498): the close of al-Tabari's argument on the one who takes refuge in the sanctuary, then the obligation of pilgrimage and the differing views on what the way to it means: provisions and a mount (reports 7474-7491, with the Prophet-attributed reports from Ibn Umar, al-Hasan and Ali and the editor's gradings of their chains), ability to reach it (7492-7496), health (7497) and the opening of Ibn Zayd's report (7498), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passage 3:97 (volume 7, pp. 29-36, reports 7454-7473): al-Tabari's summary of the meaning of the signs, the views on the security of the one who enters the sanctuary (the Jahiliyya reading, reports 7454-7458 of Qatada, Mujahid, al-Hasan and Ata; the conditional reading and the reports of Ibn Abbas, Ibn al-Zubayr, Ibn Umar, Ata, Ubayd ibn Umayr, al-Shabi, Said ibn Jubayr, Amr ibn Dinar and al-Suddi on one who incurs a penalty and takes refuge, 7459-7471; the view of security from the Fire, 7472), the editor's long note on the chain of 7472, and al-Tabari's choice with his argument from agreement of the predecessors and the report of the sanctity of Medina (7473), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:96 and 3:97 (volume 7, pp. 22-28, reports 7434-7453): al-Tabari's reading of the earliest sanctuary and the report of Abu Dharr on the first mosques (7434) with the editor's tracings, the meaning of Bakka as the place of crowding at the circumambulation and the reports of Abu Malik, Ibrahim, Abu Jafar, Mujahid, Said, Ibn al-Zubayr, Qatada, Atiyya, Ibn Shihab, Ata, Damra and al-Dahhak (7435-7447), al-Tabari's grammatical account of the accusative of the word for blessing, and, in 3:97, the plural and singular readings of the word for signs, the views on what the signs are (reports 7448-7453 of Ibn Abbas, Qatada, Mujahid, al-Hasan, al-Suddi) and his choice of the plural with the standing-place of Abraham as one of them, given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:93 to 3:96 (volume 7, pp. 15-21, reports 7419-7433): the close of 3:93 (report 7419 of Mujahid on livestock flesh, al-Tabari's choice of the report of Ibn Abbas on the veins and camel flesh and milk, report 7420 of Ibn Abbas on the Jews questioning the Prophet, his reading of the challenge to produce the Torah and the editor's notes), 3:94 (report 7421 of al-Sha'bi), 3:95 (al-Tabari's reading of the call to follow the creed of Abraham and of what is meant by the polytheists) and the start of 3:96 (the views on the first house, reports 7422-7427 of Ali, al-Hasan, Matar and Said, and the view that it was created before the earth, reports 7428-7433 of Abd Allah ibn Amr, Mujahid, al-Suddi and Qatada), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passage 3:93 (volume 7, pp. 7-14, reports 7399-7418): the start of volume 7 of al-Tabari's commentary in the Shakir edition (the page offset recorded as zero in the library note), with al-Tabari's account that the food of the descendants of Israel was lawful before the Torah except what Israel forbade himself, the three views on whether the Torah itself forbade it (reports of al-Suddi, al-Dahhak, Ibn Abbas and Qatada, the editor's notes on the wording and on the exception), al-Tabari's choice, and the reports on what Israel forbade himself, the veins (reports of Yusuf ibn Mahak, Abu Mijlaz, Qatada, Said ibn Jubayr and Mujahid) and the camel's flesh and milk (reports of Ibn Jurayj, al-Hasan and Ibn Abbas), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:91 and 3:92 (volume 6, pp. 587-592, reports 7386-7398, the last passage of volume 6): the close of 3:91 at p. 586 and 3:92 in full (al-Tabari's sense of birr as the Lord's favour of Paradise, the reports of Amr ibn Maymun and al-Suddi, his sense of spending from what one loves and of God's knowledge of it, the reports of Mujahid on Umar and the freed slave girl, of Anas on Abu Talha and his garden with the editor's long references to the collections and his note on the place-name and its readings, of Abu Dharr, and of Amr ibn Dinar and Mamar on Zayd and his horse), given as transmitted without a museum verdict. Volume 6 ends at p. 592; volume 7 begins at 3:93.
- Passages 3:90 and 3:91 (volume 6, pp. 579-586, reports 7373-7385): the close of 3:90 (the four views on the increase in disbelief, reports of Qatada, Ata al-Khurasani, Abu al-Aliya, Ikrima, Ibn Jurayj and al-Suddi, with the editor's notes on names and readings; al-Tabari's choice and his argument about the acceptance of repentance while life remains; and his sense of the word for those who have gone astray, with the editor's note on a reading of the manuscript) and 3:91 (the unbelievers who die in that state, al-Tabari's explanation of the ransom, report 7384 of Anas ibn Malik with the editor's references to the two Sahih collections, report 7385 of al-Hasan, and the grammarians' accounts of the construction of the word for gold), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:85 to 3:90 (volume 6, pp. 571-578, reports 7356-7373): the close of 3:85 (reports of Ikrima and Ibn Abbas on the communities claiming to be Muslim and the pilgrimage, with the editor's note on al-Qa'nabi), 3:86 (the two views on whom the verse concerns, al-Harith ibn Suwayd and the men who turned back from Islam in reports 7360-7367, the People of the Book in reports 7368-7371, al-Tabari's reconciling choice with the editor's praise, and his explanation of the clauses), 3:87, 3:88 (with the editor's long note on the construction and al-Tabari's own earlier words), 3:89 (the exception of those who repent) and the start of 3:90 (the view that it concerns the Jews and Christians, reports 7372-7373), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:82, 3:83, 3:84 and 3:85 (volume 6, pp. 563-570, reports 7341-7355): the close of 3:82 (report 7341 and al-Tabari's remark that the two verses address the Jews around the Prophet's place of emigration), 3:83 (the three readings of the pronoun person, al-Tabari's choice, the sense of submitting by choice or under compulsion in seven views with their reports from Mujahid, Abu al-Aliya, Ibn Abbas, al-Sha'bi, al-Hasan, Matar al-Warraq, Qatada, with the editor's notes on Jabir ibn Yazid al-Ju'fi and on a corrected wording), 3:84 (al-Tabari's explanation of the confession of faith in the earlier prophets and scriptures) and the start of 3:85 (the sense of seeking another religion, and the report that each community claimed to be Muslim and was told to perform the pilgrimage), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:81 and 3:82 (volume 6, pp. 555-562, reports 7326-7340): the rest of the dispute over whom the covenant was taken from (reports of Ibn Abbas, Ibn Tawus, Ali with the editor's note on Sayf ibn Umar, Qatada, al-Suddi, al-Hasan, Ibn Ishaq), the view that it was taken from the prophets and their nations, al-Tabari's choice and his answer to the argument of al-Rabi ibn Anas, the report of al-Suddi and al-Tabari's linguistic answer to it, the clauses of acknowledgement and of witnessing (report 7338) and the start of 3:82 on those who turn away (reports 7339-7340, with the editor's note on the words 'meaning al-Razi'), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:79, 3:80 and 3:81 (volume 6, pp. 547-554, reports 7322-7325): the close of 3:79 at p. 546, 3:80 (the two readings of the verb of command, the report attributed to Ibn Masud and al-Tabari's judgement of its chain with the editor's notes on Hajjaj ibn Muhammad and Harun ibn Musa, al-Tabari's choice and sense, report 7322 of Ibn Jurayj) and the start of 3:81 (the covenant of the prophets, the readings of the particle and the verb, the grammarians of Basra and Kufa, al-Tabari's choice, reports 7323-7325 of Mujahid and al-Rabi on the view that the covenant was taken from the People of the Book, with the editor's long note on variant readings), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passage 3:79 (volume 6, pp. 539-546, reports 7296-7321): the occasions of revelation as transmitted (the report of Ibn Ishaq from Ibn Abbas on Abu Rafi' and the Najran Christian al-Rabis with the editor's identification and his note on the spelling, the reports of Qatada, al-Rabi and Ibn Jurayj), the sense of the word rabbaniyyun in the reports of Abu Razin, al-Hasan, Mujahid, Qatada, al-Suddi, Ibn Abbas, Said ibn Jubayr, al-Dahhak and Ibn Zayd, al-Tabari's own choice of the sense with the editor's praise, and the two readings of the verb of teaching and study with al-Tabari's choice, given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:77, 3:78 and 3:79 (volume 6, pp. 532-538, reports 7282-7295): the close of 3:77 (the editor's finish on report 7281 and his choice of reading, report 7282 of Ibn Masud and al-Ashath with the editor's tracing, reports 7283-7289 on the swearing of false oaths including the report of Imran ibn Husayn, the editor's notes that two chains are disconnected and his study of the oath of compulsion), 3:78 (al-Tabari on the group who twist their tongues over the book, reports 7290-7295, the root meaning of twisting with the editor's notes on verses of Far'an ibn al-A'raf and of Majnun Banu Amir) and the start of 3:79 (al-Tabari on the sense of the human being and of the book, judgement and prophethood), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:75, 3:76 and 3:77 (volume 6, pp. 524-531, reports 7274-7281): the end of 3:75 (report 7274 of Ibn Abbas on taking chickens and sheep from the property of protected non-Muslims, with the editor's long tracing and his restoration of an unreadable word; al-Tabari on lying against God knowingly, reports 7275-7276), 3:76 (al-Tabari on fulfilling the covenant and fearing God, report 7277 of Ibn Abbas) and 3:77 (al-Tabari on those who sell God's covenant and their oaths for a small price, his comment on each of the clauses of the threat with the editor's note on a verse of Shumayr ibn al-Harith al-Dabbi, the two views on the occasion, the report of al-Ashath ibn Qays and Abdallah ibn Masud, reports 7278-7280 with the editor's biographical and tracing notes, and report 7281 begun with the editor's grading note), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:73, 3:74 and 3:75 (volume 6, pp. 516-523, reports 7255-7273): the end of al-Tabari's choice among the four views on 3:73 (with the editor's note that the gap on p. 515 is certain), his comment on the close of 3:73 (report 7255), 3:74 (on the verb for singling out, the meaning of mercy as Islam, the Quran and prophethood, reports 7256-7260, and the editor's added word in the comment on great bounty) and 3:75 (al-Tabari on the trustworthy and the faithless among the People of the Book and why God reports it, the two views on the clause about standing over the debtor, reports 7261-7265 and his choice, and the claim of no blame regarding the unlettered, reports 7266-7273, including the report of Said ibn Jubayr with the editor's note that it is raised but disconnected), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:72 and 3:73 (volume 6, pp. 509-515, reports 7238-7254): the close of 3:72 (al-Tabari on why the first part of the day is called its face, with the editor's note on a verse of Rabi ibn Ziyad about vengeance for Malik ibn Zuhayr, and reports 7238-7245 on the meaning of first and last of the day and of the hope that they may turn back) and the start of 3:73 (al-Tabari on the clause about trusting only those who follow one's religion, reports 7246-7248, and his survey of four views on the clauses about guidance, someone else being granted the like of what the Jews were granted, and disputing before their Lord, reports 7249-7254, with the editor's long notes on variant readings and on a lacuna in al-Tabari's statement of his own choice on p. 515), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:70, 3:71 and 3:72 (volume 6, pp. 502-508, reports 7219-7237): the close of 3:70 (reports 7219-7222 on the People of the Book who confirm the description of Muhammad in their books yet reject him, with the editor's restoration of a dropped link in the chain of 7222), 3:71 (al-Tabari on the intermingling of the genuine with the false, reports 7223-7227 including the report of Ibn Abbas on three men proposing to profess belief at dawn and retract at dusk, and the editor's notes on a corrupt reading in 7225; then the hiding of the matter of Muhammad, reports 7228-7230) and the start of 3:72 (the two views on the plan of a group to profess belief at the opening of the day and retract at its end, reports 7231-7237, with the editor's correction of the village name in 7233), given as transmitted without a museum verdict. The English and Urdu prose of 3:63-3:70 was also hand-checked for echoes of the verses' own wording (3:63-3:70, 9:29, 5:72, 4:171) and the hits in 3:64, 3:65, 3:67, 3:68, 3:69 and 3:70 were paraphrased.
- Passages 3:67, 3:68, 3:69 and 3:70 (volume 6, pp. 496-502, reports 7214-7218, 7219 begun): the close of 3:67 (the editor's tracing of report 7213 to al-Bukhari, and his long note on the manuscript's change of transmitter at this point to Abu Bakr Muhammad ibn Dawud ibn Sulayman, with the biography he found), 3:68 (al-Tabari on who is nearest to Abraham, reports 7214-7218 including the report of Ibn Masud with the editor's long study of its connected and disconnected chains), 3:69 (al-Tabari on the wish of a group of the People of the Book to lead the believers astray, the sense of leading astray as destruction, and the editor's discussion of a line of al-Nabigha and the readings of al-Asmai and Abu Ubayda) and the start of 3:70 (al-Tabari on the rebuke of both peoples, report 7219 begun), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:64, 3:65, 3:66 and 3:67 (volume 6, pp. 488-495, reports 7199-7213): the close of 3:64 (report 7199 on the word being the declaration of God's oneness, al-Tabari on the grammar of the clause about worshipping none but God and on what it means for people to take one another as lords, with report 7200 of Ibn Jurayj and report 7201 of Ikrima on prostration, and his meaning of the call to bear witness to submission), 3:65 (the dispute over Abraham between Jews and Christians, reports 7202-7207 including the report of Ibn Abbas, with the editor's notes), 3:66 (what the two peoples knew and what they did not, reports 7208-7210 and the editor's notes on a lost line in the manuscript) and 3:67 (al-Tabari on Abraham being neither Jew nor Christian, reports 7211-7212, and report 7213 on Zayd ibn Amr ibn Nufayl and the two scholars, with the editor's comparison with the version of al-Bukhari), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:63 and 3:64 (volume 6, pp. 479-487, reports 7181-7198): the rest of report 7181 (the counsel of al-Aqib, the delegation's withdrawal and its request for an arbiter, with the editor's notes on the word for a satisfactory man and on a variant of Ibn Hisham), reports 7182-7190 on who the Prophet brought to the mutual invocation and what would have followed had the delegation come out (including the settlement terms in the report of al-Suddi and the editor's remarks on the chains and on the reports being mostly mursal), and, under 3:64, al-Tabari's meaning of the verse, the reports 7191-7196 on whether it concerned the Jews of Medina or the Najran delegation, his reasoning that it addresses both peoples of the Book, his explanation of the verb of summoning, the grammarians' discussion of the word for 'just' and its case, and reports 7197-7198, given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:59, 3:60, 3:61, 3:62 and 3:63 (volume 6, pp. 470-479, reports 7163-7180, 7181 begun): the close of 3:59 (the end of report 7163 and reports 7164-7166 on the Najran delegation, with the editor's notes on the manuscript and Ibn Hisham, and al-Tabari's grammatical answer on why the clause about Adam is not a qualifier and on the tense of the closing verb), 3:60 (reports 7167-7170 on doubt), 3:61 (al-Tabari on the invitation to mutual invocation, the editor's long note on a line of Labid, and reports 7171-7175 including the report of Abdullah ibn al-Harith on the Prophet's remark about the people of Najran), 3:62 (reports 7176-7179) and 3:63 (al-Tabari on the turning away, report 7180 of al-Shabi on the Najran delegation's refusal of mutual invocation and its agreement to pay tribute, with the editor's notes, and report 7181 begun), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:55, 3:56, 3:57, 3:58 and 3:59 (volume 6, pp. 459-469, reports 7145-7162, 7163 begun): the rest of 3:55 (report 7145 of Abu Hurayra on the prophets as half-brothers, the nearness of Jesus to the Prophet and his descent, with the editor's grading of the chain as very weak and his statement that the base of the report is sound by other chains; al-Tabari's argument that a second death cannot be joined to the first; reports 7146-7148 of Ibn Ishaq and al-Hasan on the clause of purification; reports 7149-7155 on the followers placed above the deniers, with the second view that Christians are meant above Jews; and the shift of address in the close of the verse with the editor's note and al-Tabari's parallel at 10:22), 3:56 and 3:57 (al-Tabari on the two groups, report 7156 of Ibn Abbas, and the editor's notes on manuscript readings), 3:58 (reports 7157-7159 on the recitation and the wise remembrance) and the start of 3:59 (al-Tabari on the likeness of Jesus and Adam, reports 7160-7162 on the Najran delegation and the begun report 7163), given as transmitted without a museum verdict. A guessed cross-reference to 2:15 in the 3:54 entry (p. 454) is also reworded, since the editor's footnote number is unreadable in the scan.
- Passages 3:52, 3:53, 3:54 and 3:55 (volume 6, pp. 451-458, reports 7129-7144): the close of 3:52 (al-Tabari on Islam as the religion of Jesus and the earlier prophets and the report 7129 of Ibn Ishaq), 3:53 (the disciples' prayer as al-Tabari reads it, report 7130), 3:54 (the plotting of the unbelievers among the Israelites, the reports 7131 and 7132 of al-Suddi on the likeness cast on a follower, with the cross-reference to 4:157 and 61:14, and the second sense al-Tabari allows for God's plan, where an earlier guessed cross-reference to 2:15 is removed because the editor's footnote number is unreadable in the scan) and the start of 3:55 (the three views on the meaning of the word for taking back, the reports 7133-7143 of al-Rabi, Matar al-Warraq, al-Hasan, Ibn Jurayj, Kab al-Ahbar, Ibn Zayd, Ibn Abbas and Wahb ibn Munabbih with the editor's gradings, al-Tabari's choice and report 7144 on the descent of Jesus with the editor's tracing in Ahmad), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:50, 3:51 and 3:52 (volume 6, pp. 440-451, reports 7115-7128): correction of the chain of report 7115 in the 3:50 files (the previous entry had put an inferred name in brackets after 'Muhammad ibn'; the printed text only breaks off at the end of p. 439 and p. 440 opens with the words 'Jafar ibn al-Zubayr', so the files now state exactly that and nothing is inferred), the end of 3:50 (reports 7116-7118 of al-Hasan and Mujahid on what was made lawful and on the sign, al-Tabari's reading of the call to fear God and obey), 3:51 (report 7119 of Ibn Ishaq, the two readings of the particle opening the clause, al-Tabari's choice of the commonly read form on the ground of the readers' agreement, and his remark that the verse is proof against the Najran delegation), and the start of 3:52 (the senses of the verb for perceiving, with the editor's notes on the Kumayt line, the meaning of the preposition for 'with', the reports of al-Suddi and Ibn Jurayj, the long story of Jesus and the king, the Jew and the loaves with the editor's textual notes, the two accounts of why he sought helpers, the reports of Said ibn Jubayr, Abu Arta, Qatada and al-Dahhak on the name of the disciples, al-Tabari's choice, the saying on every prophet's disciple with the editor's grading and the Abu Jilda note), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:49 and 3:50 (volume 6, pp. 431-439, reports 7098-7115): the close of 3:49 (al-Tabari's argument for the born-blind and against the weak-sighted view, the raising of the dead by supplication and the report of Wahb ibn Munabbih, the telling of what is eaten and stored, al-Tabari's distinction between prophets and astrologers and soothsayers, the reports of Ibn Ishaq, al-Suddi, Said ibn Jubayr, Mujahid, Ata, al-Rabi and al-Hasan on the child Jesus and the boys, the reports of Qatada and Ammar on the table, the grammar of the verb for storing with the editor's notes on Zuhayr, and the closing sense of the sign) and the start of 3:50 (the accusative of the word for confirming, the Torah and Jesus' practice with the reports of Wahb, Qatada, al-Rabi and Ibn Jurayj on what was made lawful), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:48 and 3:49 (volume 6, pp. 422-430, reports 7080-7097): the close of 3:48 (the readers who use the first person plural, al-Tabari's view that the two readings agree in sense, the book, wisdom, the Torah and the Gospel with the reports of Ibn Jurayj, Qatada and Ibn Ishaq and the editor's note on the Sira) and the first part of 3:49 (the messenger to the Israelites, the sign and the readings of the word for bird with al-Tabari's preference for the written form of the codex, the accounts of the clay bird in the reports of Ibn Ishaq and Ibn Jurayj, the grammar of blowing into it with the editor's notes on the poets Ibn Mufarrigh and Suwayd, the senses of healing and of the word for one born blind or night-blind with the reports of Mujahid, Qatada, Ibn Abbas, al-Suddi, al-Hasan and Ikrima), given as transmitted without a museum verdict. Report 7097 is printed as 7017 in the OCR and numbered 7097 from context.
- Passages 3:45 to 3:48 (volume 6, pp. 413-421, reports 7063-7079): the close of 3:45 (the Kufan and Basran grammarians on the masculine pronoun, the sonship claims and the slander denied, the sense of the Messiah with the reports of Ibrahim and Said and the editor's note on a gap in the text, the senses of eminent and of those brought near with the reports of Ibn Ishaq, Qatada and al-Rabi), all of 3:46 (the infant's speech and the grown age, the reports of Ibn Abbas, Ibn Jurayj, Mujahid, al-Hasan and Ibn Zayd, the Najran delegation, and the editor's notes on the poets), all of 3:47 (Mary's question and the answer, with the report of Ibn Ishaq) and the start of 3:48 (the readers' difference over the verb for teaching), given as transmitted without a museum verdict. Report 7077 is printed as 7017 in the OCR and numbered 7077 from context.
- Passages 3:43, 3:44 and 3:45 (volume 6, pp. 404-412, reports 7052-7062): the close of 3:43 (bowing and prostration as humble submission, and al-Tabari's reading of the command), all of 3:44 (the accounts of the unseen as proof of prophethood against the People of the Two Books, the senses of revelation with the editor's notes on the poets al-Ajjaj, Kab ibn Zuhayr and Ruba, the casting of pens with the reports of Qatada, Mujahid, Ibn Abbas, al-Dahhak, al-Hasan and Ibn Ishaq, and al-Tabari's grammar of the interrogative) and the start of 3:45 (glad tidings and the Word from God, the views that it is the divine command or the name of Jesus with the reports of Qatada and Ibn Abbas, and al-Tabari's choice with the grammatical argument for the masculine pronoun), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:42 and 3:43 (volume 6, pp. 395-403, reports 7028-7051): the close of 3:42 (the three-part report of Qatada on the women who suffice and on the best of camel-riding women, with the editor's tracing in Ahmad, al-Hakim, al-Tirmidhi, Ibn Hibban, Muslim and al-Bukhari and his grading of its wordings; the report of Abu Hurayra; the four best women through Thabit al-Bunani and Anas; the report of Abu Musa al-Ashari; the report of Fatima bint al-Husayn on the final conversation of Fatima with her father, with the editor's note on the break in its chain and on the age ascribed to Jesus; the report on Khadija; the sayings of Mujahid and Ibn Jurayj on being purified and preferred; and the report of Ibn Ishaq on Mary, Joseph and the angels) and the start of 3:43 (the three views on the command to devotion: prolonged standing with the reports of Mujahid, al-Rabi and al-Awzai, sincere devotion, and obedience with the reports of Qatada, al-Suddi, Abu Said al-Khudri through Ibn Lahia and al-Hasan), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:41 and 3:42 (volume 6, pp. 385-394, reports 7005-7027): the close of 3:41 (the sign as a seizing of the tongue and al-Tabari's word on his slip, the reports of Qatada, al-Rabi and Jubayr ibn Nufayr with the editor's notes on the transmitters, the case for the future particle, the senses of the word for a sign by gesture with the lines of Juayya ibn Ayidh, the two views of the commentators with the reports of Mujahid, al-Dahhak, Ibn Abbas, Ibn Ishaq, Ibn Zayd, Qatada, al-Rabi, al-Suddi, Abdullah ibn Kathir and al-Hasan, the command to remember much, the reports of Muhammad ibn Kab, the senses of evening and early morning with the editor's notes on Humayd ibn Thawr, Umar ibn Abi Rabia and Jarir, and the reports of Mujahid) and the start of 3:42 (the senses of chosen, purified and preferred over the women of her time, the report of Ali and of Abdullah ibn Jafar on the best women, with the editor's tracing of the hadith in Ahmad, al-Bukhari, Muslim, al-Tirmidhi and al-Hakim), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:39, 3:40 and 3:41 (volume 6, pp. 377-385, reports 6980-7004): the close of 3:39 (the word for one who abstains: Jarir's line and the editor's note on its variants, the reports of Abdullah ibn Masud, Ibn al-As through Said ibn al-Musayyib in its raised and stopped forms with the editor's note on Ibn Kathir and al-Suyuti, Said ibn Jubayr, Mujahid, al-Raqashi, al-Dahhak, Qatada, Ibn Abbas, Ibn Zayd, al-Suddi and al-Hasan; the sense of prophet and of the righteous, and the editor's note on the division of the old manuscript at p. 380); 3:40 (Zechariah's question, the word for a barren person and the line of Amir ibn al-Tufayl with the editor's notes, the sense of the verb of reaching, the question whether the prophet doubted and the reports of al-Suddi and Ikrima on the whisper, al-Tabari's two answers, the sense of 'thus' and the report of al-Suddi); and 3:41 (the request for a sign, the report of al-Suddi, the differing explanations of the form of the word for sign with the editor's notes, and the start of al-Tabari's comment on the sign), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passage 3:39 (volume 6, pp. 368-376, reports 6947-6979): the close of al-Tabari's case on the particle after the call; the readings of the verb of glad tidings (the doubled form and the light form, the line of the Yamama dialect, the lines of Abd Qays ibn Khafaf al-Burjumi and Jarir, the report of Muadh al-Kufi, and al-Tabari's reply that the two forms differ in no sense), the name of John (reports of Qatada), the confirming of the Word (reports of Mujahid, al-Raqashi, Qatada, al-Rabi, al-Suddi, al-Dahhak, Ibn Abbas through three chains and al-Hasan, the account of the two mothers, and al-Tabari's reply to a Basran grammarian with the editor's remark), the word for 'master' (the reports of Qatada, Said ibn Jubayr, Mujahid, al-Raqashi, al-Dahhak, Sufyan, Ibn Zayd, Said ibn al-Musayyib, Ibn Abbas and Ikrima) and the start of the account of the word for one who abstains, with the editor's notes on al-Akhtal's line, given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:37, 3:38 and 3:39 (volume 6, pp. 358-367, reports 6937-6946): the close of 3:37 (the plural of the word for the sanctuary, Zechariah's question and Mary's answer, the seven doors, the report of Ibn Abbas through his family chain, al-Tabari on provision without reckoning and the editor's filling of a blank in the manuscript); 3:38 (the sense of the word for 'there', Zechariah's hope for a child on seeing fruit out of season, the report of al-Suddi, Ikrima and Abu Bakr, Ibn Ishaq, the senses of offspring, blessed and 'from you', why the singular is used with a feminine form and the editor's notes on the lines of poetry cited, the sense of the divine name of hearing and a view attributed to a Basran grammarian); and the start of 3:39 (the two readings of the verb of the call, the reading of Ibn Masud, the report of al-Suddi, al-Tabari's reply on the plural used of one, his choice of the group of angels, the time of the call, and the readings of the particle that follows with al-Tabari's preference), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passage 3:37 (volume 6, pp. 348-357, reports 6902-6935 and the following reports): the casting of pens at the Jordan in the reports of Ikrima, al-Rabi, al-Suddi, Mujahid, Qatada, Ikrima and Abu Bakr, Ibn Abbas through Said ibn Jubayr, Ibn Ishaq through Muhammad ibn Jafar ibn al-Zubayr and al-Hasan, with the editor's notes on the manuscript and on the sisters married to Zechariah and Imran; the view that Zechariah took Mary without lots (the name of the mother of John, the reports through Shu'ayb al-Jaba'i and Ibn Ishaq) and al-Tabari's reason for preferring the doubled reading of the verb; the provision found with Mary (fruit out of season, grapes, the seven doors, fruit of Paradise, al-Hasan's report, and the view of a surplus beyond what the carpenter Jurayj brought, from Ibn Ishaq), with the editor's notes on Abu Ishaq al-Kufi and on the name of al-Suddi's narrator; the start of al-Tabari's account of the word for the sanctuary, given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:36 and 3:37 (volume 6, pp. 340-348, reports 6893-6901): the close of the reports on Satan's touch of the newborn (Ibn Abbas, Wahb ibn Munabbih's account of the devils and the birth of Jesus, the mursal reports of Qatada and al-Rabi, and the reports of Abu Hurayra through al-A'raj and Abu Salama), with the editor's extended notes on transmitters, on al-Bukhari, Muslim and Ahmad, on a reading of Ibn Kathir's phrasing and on a report from Ahmad he supplies from the Musnad; 3:37 (acceptance of Mary and the form of the verbal noun, Abu Amr ibn al-Ala on the vowel of the word for acceptance; the growth of Mary; the two readings of the verb for guardianship, the account of the casting of arrows in the Jordan with two versions of the sign and the editor's notes on the manuscript, al-Tabari's choice of the doubled form, his reply to the argument from another verse, the readings and case of the name Zechariah, the sense of guardianship and the line of poetry cited), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:35 and 3:36 (volume 6, pp. 331-339, reports 6868-6892): 3:35 (the reports of Said ibn Jubayr, Qatada, al-Suddi, al-Rabi, al-Dahhak, Ikrima and al-Hasan on what it meant that Imran's wife freed her unborn child: that only males were dedicated, that the dedicated person served and swept the church, her age and barrenness, the editor's note on the word for letting loose with a cross-reference to Surat al-Ma'ida); 3:36 (the feminine pronoun and the meaning of giving birth; the two readings of the clause on God's knowledge and al-Tabari's choice of the widespread one; her apology that a male is stronger for the service and that a female is hindered by menstruation and childbirth; the sense of seeking refuge, and the reports that Satan touches every newborn except Mary and her son, from Abu Hurayra and Ibn Ishaq's chain, Ibn Wahb, al-Zuhri, Abd al-Razzaq and al-A'mash, with the editor's extensive notes on the transmitters and on al-Bukhari, Muslim, Ahmad and al-Hakim), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:31 to 3:35 (volume 6, pp. 322-331, reports 6845-6867): 3:31 (the two views on the occasion: a people who said they love God, reports from al-Hasan and Ibn Jurayj, against the Najran delegation's honouring of Jesus in the report of Muhammad ibn Jafar ibn al-Zubayr; al-Tabari's choice of the second and his reasons, the editor's notes on manuscript readings); 3:32 (the address to the Najran delegation and the Gospel in their hands, with the continuation of the Ibn Ishaq report); 3:33 (the chosen houses of Abraham and Imran taken as their believers, reports of Ibn Abbas, Qatada and al-Hasan); 3:34 (the offspring joined in religion, grammar of the accusative, the comparison with two verses of Surat al-Tawba); 3:35 (the wife of Imran, her name and lineage as reported by Ibn Ishaq with the editor's notes on the variant forms of the names, the grammar of the word for freed and the editor's defence of al-Tabari's reading against the printed text, the occasion of her vow, the reports of Mujahid and al-Shabi on its meaning), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:28 to 3:30 (volume 6, pp. 314-321, reports 6829-6844): the meaning of precaution toward unbelievers as the tongue only, as sparing Muslim blood and property, and as conciliation and good manners (reports from Ibn Abbas, Ikrima, Mujahid, Abu al-Aliyah and al-Dahhak), the second view that it means keeping ties of kinship (Qatada, al-Hasan) and al-Tabari's reasons for judging it less apparent, the two readings of the word for precaution; the warning of the Day of return; 3:29 (what is concealed or shown in the hearts and God's knowledge and power); 3:30 (the day each soul finds its deeds present, the grammarians' view on the omitted verb and on the relative pronoun, the word for term with the editor's note on a poem of al-Tirimmah and on al-Tabari's reading of his diwan, reports from Qatada, al-Suddi, Ibn Jurayj and al-Hasan; the warning joined to God's compassion), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:27 and 3:28 (volume 6, pp. 302-314, reports 6795-6828): the sense of making the night enter the day, with the editor's notes on its verbal nouns; the lengthening and shortening of day and night in reports from al-Suddi, Ibn Abbas, Mujahid, al-Hasan, Qatada, al-Dahhak and Ibn Zayd; the three views on bringing the living from the dead (the living creature from the lifeless seed, the date palm from the stone and the chick from the egg, the believer from the unbeliever), with the report on Adam's clay from Salman or Ibn Mas'ud, the mursal report of al-Zuhri on the Prophet's maternal aunt Khalida, and the editor's notes on transmitters; al-Tabari's choice of the first view and of the doubled reading of the word for the dead; provision without reckoning and his paraphrase of the whole verse, with the continuation of Muhammad ibn Jafar ibn al-Zubayr's report on Jesus; 3:28 (the prohibition of taking unbelievers as supporters, the exception of precaution, the reports of Ibn Abbas, al-Hasan and al-Suddi, and the account of the Ansari men and the allies of Ka'b ibn al-Ashraf), given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passages 3:23 to 3:26 (volume 6, pp. 292-301, reports 6786-6794): al-Tabari's reason why the Book of 3:23 is the Torah (they denied the Qur'an and claimed to affirm the Torah, so the argument against them is stronger); 3:24 (their refusal of the Torah's judgement because of their claim of a limited stay of forty days, the days of the calf, and their fabricated claims of being God's sons and beloved, with the editor's note on the sense of the oath-expiation phrase; reports 6786-6788 from Qatada, al-Rabi' and Mujahid); 3:25 (the threat of the gathering and the full requital, al-Tabari's answer on why the preposition lam rather than fi is used, with the editor's restoration of a corrupted sentence and his citation of al-Farra'); 3:26 (the vocative 'Allahumma': the grammarians' two views on the final mim, as a substitute for the vocative particle or as the remnant of a joined word, the Arabic verses cited, the editor's long note on a word he could not identify; the gloss of the Owner of dominion, report 6789 from Ibn Ishaq; the giving and taking of dominion with the omitted clause explained; the report from Qatada, 6790-6791, that it answered the Prophet's request for the kingdoms of Persia and Rome for his community; Mujahid's view, 6792-6793, that dominion means prophethood; the gloss of honouring and humbling, and report 6794). Reports as transmitted, no museum verdict. Source: Tafsir al-Tabari, Shakir ed., vol. 6, pp. 292-301.
- Passages 3:21 to 3:23 (volume 6, pp. 284-291, reports 6776-6785): the close of Ibn Ishaq's report 6776 (Muhammad ibn Ja'far ibn al-Zubayr) that the passage from 3:21 to 3:26 was addressed to the Jews and Christians, al-Tabari's gloss that those who killed God's messengers were those sent to forbid what they did (Zakariyya and his son Yahya among them), the reading question on the killing of those who command justice (the reading of the cities against the later Kufan reading with the sense of fighting, which he ties to a claim about Ibn Mas'ud's codex, and his choice on the grounds of the readers' agreement), reports 6777-6779 (Ma'qil ibn Abi Miskin, Qatada, Ibn Jurayj) on who those commanders of justice were, report 6780 from Abu Ubayda ibn al-Jarrah on the forty-three prophets and the one hundred and twelve devout men, with the editor's long note on the chain (Abu Ubayd al-Wusabi, Muhammad ibn Himyar, Abu al-Hasan the client of the Banu Asad); the gloss of 3:22 on the painful punishment, the ruin of works in this world and the next and the absence of helpers; and 3:23 (the Book to which they were called, the Torah, with report 6781-6782 from Ibn Abbas on the House of Study and the dispute over Abraham's religion, the editor's notes on the names Nu'aym and Nu'man; the view that it is the Book sent to Muhammad, 6783-6785 from Qatada and Ibn Jurayj, with the editor's note on the stoning case at Khaybar; and al-Tabari's choice that the verse does not fix the point of dispute, with his gloss of turning away). Reports as transmitted, no museum verdict. Source: Tafsir al-Tabari, Shakir ed., vol. 6, pp. 284-291.
- Passages 3:19 to 3:21 (volume 6, pp. 275-283, reports 6764-6776): the close of Abu al-Aliyah's report on Islam as sincerity to God alone (6764), Ibn Zayd's gloss of the word for having submitted in al-Hujurat as entering peace and leaving war (6765) and Ibn Ishaq's report from Muhammad ibn Ja'far ibn al-Zubayr (6766); the second part of 3:19 on the people given the Book who differed only after knowledge came to them, al-Tabari's reading that they knew the truth about Jesus and differed through aggression and the search for rule, reports 6767 (Abu al-Aliyah via al-Rabi), 6768 (Ibn Umar), the long report 6769 on Moses and the seventy rabbis (al-Rabi ibn Anas), al-Tabari's remark that al-Rabi took it to mean the Jews and the other view that it means the Christians (6770), with the editor's corrections of the printed text; the end of 3:19 on God's reckoning as swift enumeration without effort (6771-6772 from Mujahid); 3:20 (the Prophet's reply to the Najran Christians, why the face is named, the sense of 'those who follow me', 6773; the question put to the people of the Book and the unlettered and the grammatical question of a conditional after a question, with the parallels in al-Ma'ida and al-Saff, 6774-6775 on the unlettered, and the close on conveying the message); and the start of 3:21 with report 6776. Reports as transmitted, no museum verdict. Source: Tafsir al-Tabari, Shakir ed., vol. 6, pp. 275-283.
- Passages 3:18 and 3:19 (volume 6, pp. 268-275, reports 6760-6764): the close of al-Tabari's argument on the reading of 3:18 (his choice of the opening vowel on the first particle and the other on the second, report 6760 from al-Suddi that the witnessing governs the second particle, with the two grammatical possibilities he draws from it for the first, and the editor's notes on the scribe's slips); the case of the word for 'maintaining' (the Basran view that it is a circumstantial of the pronoun, the Kufan view that it is a circumstantial of God's name, a reading ascribed to Ibn Mas'ud, and his own choice, with the editor's note on his term for it and on al-Farra); the glosses on justice (reports 6761 from Ibn Ishaq and 6762 from Mujahid), on the Mighty and the Wise; his reading of the verse as addressed to the Christians who disputed with the Prophet about Jesus and to polytheists, with the parallel from al-Anfal (8:41), his rejection of the Basran sense 'decreed' (identified by the editor as aimed at Abu Ubayda); and, at 3:19, his gloss of the word for religion as obedience and humility, with lines of an unnamed poet, al-Qutami and al-A'sha as described and annotated by the editor, his gloss of the word for submission, and reports 6763 (Qatada) and 6764 (Abu al-Aliyah, begun). Reports as transmitted, no museum verdict. Source: Tafsir al-Tabari, Shakir ed., vol. 6, pp. 268-275.
- Passages 3:15 to 3:18 (volume 6, pp. 261-268, reports 6751-6759): the close of al-Tabari's comment on 3:15 (the purified companions, whom he says were purified of every impurity of the women of this world as he had explained earlier; the word for divine good pleasure as a verbal noun with its readings, the one with the dammed first letter being the dialect of Qays and Asim's reading; report 6751 from Jabir on God's pleasure as the highest rank of the people of Paradise, with the editor's note on the sound report from Abu Said al-Khudri in al-Bukhari and Ibn Hajar's mention of Jabir's in the Fath; and the sense of God being All-Seeing of His servants, both those who fear Him and those who prefer the desires of this world); 3:16 (the two cases open to the relative clause, citing the parallel in al-Tawba, 9:111-112, and al-Farra; the believers' supplication for forgiveness and protection from the Fire, with al-Tabari's reason why they asked specifically for protection); 3:17 (his glosses on the patient, the truthful, the obedient and the spenders, report 6752 from Qatada, and the genitive case of the whole series); the interpreters' three views on seeking forgiveness at the pre-dawn hours (praying then, 6753-6754 from Qatada; asking forgiveness, with the report of Ibn Mas'ud heard in the mosque, Ibn Umar and Nafi, the seventy times from Anas and Ja'far ibn Muhammad, 6755-6758; and attending the dawn prayer in congregation, 6759 from Zayd ibn Aslam) with the editor's notes on Hurayth ibn Abi Matar, on Ibrahim ibn Hatib and his father and on the Qa'nabi brothers, and al-Tabari's choice; and the beginning of 3:18 (the sense of God, the angels and the people of knowledge bearing witness, the view of some Basrans, whom the editor identifies as Abu Ubayda, that the verb means God decreed, and al-Tabari's rejection of the reading of a later grammarian, identified by the editor as al-Kisa'i, who read both particles with the same vowel). Reports as transmitted, no museum verdict. Source: Tafsir al-Tabari, Shakir ed., vol. 6, pp. 261-268.
- Passages 3:14 and 3:15 (volume 6, pp. 251-261, reports 6729-6750): the interpreters' four views on the word for the marked horses (grazing, with Said ibn Jubayr, Ibn Abbas, al-Hasan, al-Rabi and Mujahid in one report; well-formed and handsome, with Mujahid and Ikrima; marked with the blazes and patches of the coat, with Ibn Abbas and Qatada; and those made ready for jihad, with Ibn Zayd), al-Tabari's choice of 'marked' as the Arabs use the word for making a sign, with his reading of the opposing view from the verb for sending cattle to pasture and a line of al-Akhtal, the lines of al-Nabigha and Labid, the editor's notes on the narrators (Abu Abd al-Rahman al-Muqri, Bashir ibn Abi Amr al-Khawlani), on the poems and on the anecdote of Shaddad ibn al-Mundhir; his glosses on the cattle, the crops, the demonstrative that stands for the whole list, the 'provision of this world' and the verbal noun of return (report 6750, al-Suddi); the question of how the verse can speak of a good return when punishment is also with God, and 3:15 opened with the three views of the grammarians on where the question ends and what that does to the case of the word for the gardens, with his choice. Reports as transmitted, no museum verdict. Also: a hand-grep of 3:14 for typed verse wording (one fragment reworded) and one lexical word replaced in the note on report 6710. Source: Tafsir al-Tabari, Shakir ed., vol. 6, pp. 251-261.
- Passage 3:14 (volume 6, pp. 244-250, reports 6696-6728): the interpreters' difference over the amount of the qintar in seven views (twelve hundred uqiya, with Mu'adh ibn Jabal, Ibn Umar, Asim, Abu Hurayra and a report from Ubayy ibn Ka'b; twelve hundred dinars, with al-Hasan, Ibn Abbas and al-Dahhak; twelve thousand dirhams or a thousand dinars; eighty thousand dirhams or a hundred ratl of gold, with Ibn al-Musayyab, Qatada, Abu Salih and al-Suddi; seventy thousand, with Mujahid and Ibn Umar; a bull's skin filled with gold, with Abu Nadra; and much wealth, with al-Rabi ibn Anas), al-Tabari's choice of 'much wealth' with a word from the language scholars (Abu Ubayda) that the Arabs do not fix its weight, his gloss of the doubled form (after al-Farra), the view that it means minted coin (al-Suddi), and the report from Anas on the sum named in the verse on dowries (6728), with the editor's long notes on the chains and narrators (Shababa ibn Sawwar, Mukhlad ibn Abd al-Wahid, Ata ibn Abi Maymuna, Aban ibn Abi Ayyash and others), on Ibn Kathir, al-Hakim and al-Suyuti, and on the scribe's slips. Reports as transmitted, with no museum verdict. Source: Tafsir al-Tabari, Shakir ed., vol. 6, pp. 244-250.
- Passages 3:13 close and 3:14 start (volume 6, pp. 236-244, reports 6685-6695): the reports of a polytheist force of nine hundred to a thousand (Urwa ibn al-Zubayr, 6685; Qatada, al-Rabi and Ibn Jurayj, 6686-6689) and al-Tabari's conclusion that they favour the first view on the seeing; the view that the verb of seeing turns from address to report on the absent, with the arithmetic of 'twice' (a grammarian's argument that al-Tabari takes from al-Farra), the view that the disbelievers saw the Muslims as double, the reading in the passive, al-Tabari's choice of the third-person reading with three lessenings (Ibn Mas'ud, 6690-6691), his account of why God told the Jews, the sense of the phrase for seeing with the eye, the sense of God's supporting whom He wills, and the lesson (6692-6693); and 3:14 with the purpose of the verse as a rebuke of those who preferred this world (al-Hasan, 6694; Umar, 6695). Editor's notes on text readings and on narrators included. Reports as transmitted, with no museum verdict. Source: Tafsir al-Tabari, Shakir ed., vol. 6, pp. 236-244.
- Passages 3:12 close and 3:13 (volume 6, pp. 228-236, reports 6667-6684): the Banu Qaynuqa' reports of Ibn Ishaq (6667-6669, with Finhas the Jew in 6670) and al-Tabari's conclusion that they favour the second-person reading; the gloss of 'gathered' and of the place of rest (Mujahid, 6671-6672); 3:13 on the sign in the two parties that met at Badr (6673-6680, Qatada, al-Rabi, Ibn Abbas, Ikrima, Mujahid), the nominative of the second party with the lines of Kuthayyir and of al-Najashi al-Harithi and the editor's notes on them; the two readings of the verb for seeing (Medina readers, second person; Kufa, Basra and some Meccan readers, third person), the view that the Muslims saw the polytheists as double and then as equal (Ibn Mas'ud, 6681, with the link to 8:44), the view that they saw them as they were (Ibn Abbas, 6682), al-Tabari's remark that this contradicts the reports on the polytheists' number, and the reports of a thousand (Ali, 6683; Abd Allah, 6684). Reports as transmitted, with no museum verdict. Source: Tafsir al-Tabari, Shakir ed., vol. 6, pp. 228-236.
- Passages 3:8 close to 3:12 (volume 6, pp. 218-227, reports 6657-6666): the editor's long note on the name of the Companion in report 6656 (Sabra or Samura ibn Fatik, two Companions kept apart by al-Bukhari and Ibn Abi Hatim, and his conjecture about the unnamed narrator in al-Bukhari's chain); reports 6657 (Abd Allah ibn Amr) and 6658 (Umm Salama) on the heart between two fingers of the Merciful, with the editor's references (Ahmad, Muslim, al-Ajurri, al-Bayhaqi, al-Suyuti); 3:9 as a request for firmness until the Day of gathering; 3:10 on wealth and children not availing the disbelievers; 3:11 on the sense of the word da'b in the phrase about Pharaoh's people (custom, deed or denial, reports 6659-6665, with the root, the usage of Imru' al-Qays and the editor's note on Kuthayyir); and 3:12 on the two readings of the verb about being overcome (second or third person), al-Tabari's choice of the second, and the Banu Qaynuqa' report from Ibn Abbas (6666). Reports as transmitted, with no museum verdict. Source: Tafsir al-Tabari, Shakir ed., vol. 6, pp. 218-227.
- Passages 3:7 close and 3:8 (volume 6, pp. 210-218, reports 6648-6656): the Arabic grammarians' difference over the word for 'all' when what it is annexed to is omitted (a Basran view that omission is allowed only when it is a noun, a Kufan view that adjective and noun are alike, and al-Tabari's choice of the second as closer to analogy, with the editor's note on a misreading in the print); the close of 3:7 on those who take heed (6648, Muhammad ibn Jafar ibn al-Zubayr); 3:8 as the plea of the rooted (6649), al-Tabari's argument against the Qadariyya, whom the editor identifies with the Mu'tazila, that praise of the plea shows that God's turning of a heart is just, and the reports on the Prophet's prayer about the turning of hearts (6650-6656: Umm Salama, Asma bint Yazid, Jabir, Anas, al-Nawwas ibn Sam'an and Samura ibn Fatik) with the editor's long notes on chains, narrators and collections (Ahmad, al-Tirmidhi, Ibn Majah, al-Hakim, Ibn Khuzayma, al-Ajurri, al-Bayhaqi, al-Suyuti). Reports as transmitted, with no museum verdict. Source: Tafsir al-Tabari, Shakir ed., vol. 6, pp. 210-218.
- Passage 3:7 (volume 6, pp. 204-209, reports 6637-6647): al-Tabari's account of the sense of the word for interpretation in Arabic (explanation, return, destination) with the line of al-A'sha described and the editor's long notes on its two transmitted wordings (Lisan al-Arab, al-Azhari); the sense of the rooted in knowledge as those who have mastered what they know without doubt, and the root of the word; the report of Abu al-Darda and Abu Umama (6637-6638) on who is rooted, with the editor's notes on its transmitters and sources, including his grading of one narrator; the interpreters who named the rooted by their saying that all is from their Lord (Ibn Abbas, al-Suddi, Ibn Jurayj, al-Dahhak, 6639-6642); and the gloss on 'all is from our Lord' as the firm and the resembling together (6643-6647). Reports as transmitted, with no museum verdict. Source: Tafsir al-Tabari, Shakir ed., vol. 6, pp. 204-209.
- Passage 3:7 (volume 6, pp. 199-204, reports 6623-6636): the close of al-Tabari's reasoning on the aim of the people of inclination; the three views on the sense of the interpretation they sought (the term of Islam, Ibn Abbas, 6623; the outcomes of the Qur'an and the coming of the abrogating, al-Suddi, 6624; reading the resembling to fit the error in their hearts, Muhammad ibn Jafar ibn al-Zubayr, 6625) and his choice; his gloss on what only God knows; the interpreters' two readings of the sentence structure, that the rooted in knowledge begin a new sentence (Aisha, Ibn Abbas, Urwa, Abu Nahik, Umar ibn Abd al-Aziz, Malik, 6626-6631) or share in knowing it (Ibn Abbas, Mujahid, al-Rabi', Ibn Ishaq, 6632-6636), the grammatical positions of the Basrans and Kufans, his choice, and the readings attributed to Ubayy and Abd Allah ibn Masud, with the editor's notes on the manuscript and on Ibn Hisham's text; and the start of his account of the Arabic word for interpretation. Reports as transmitted, with no museum verdict. Source: Tafsir al-Tabari, Shakir ed., vol. 6, pp. 199-204.
- Passage 3:7 (volume 6, pp. 191-198, reports 6607-6622): the editor's remaining notes on Matar al-Warraq and Ibn Hibban's wording; the further transmissions of the hadith of Aisha about those who dispute over the resembling verses, with the chains al-Tabari gives (6607-6615) and the editor's gradings of the narrators and his tracing through Ibn Majah, al-Bukhari, Muslim, Abu Dawud, al-Tirmidhi, Ibn Hibban, Ibn Kathir and al-Suyuti, including the question whether al-Qasim ibn Muhammad stands between Ibn Abi Mulayka and Aisha; al-Tabari's judgement on whom the verse first concerns; the interpreters on the aim of the people of inclination (idolatry, the doubts, confusion; 6616-6621) and his choice; and his extension of the verse to every innovator, with Ibn Abbas on the Khawarij (6622) and the editor's notes. Reports as transmitted, with no museum verdict. Source: Tafsir al-Tabari, Shakir ed., vol. 6, pp. 191-198.
- Passage 3:7 (volume 6, pp. 183-190, reports 6591-6606): the rest of the readers' views on the foundation of the Book (the sura openings, Abu Fakhita, 6591, with the editor's note on al-Suyuti's merged version); al-Tabari's explanation of the inclination in hearts with the lexical forms and the reports of doubt (6592-6597); the following of the resembling verses (Ibn Abbas, Muhammad ibn Jafar ibn al-Zubayr, Mujahid, al-Suddi on the abrogating and the abrogated, 6598-6601); the three views on who is meant: the Najran delegation (al-Rabi', 6602), the Jewish men who tried to calculate the term of Islam from the letters, and every innovator, with Qatada's long statement against the Haruriyya and the Saba'iyya as he reported it and the editor's notes on the groups and on Badr and al-Hudaybiya (6603-6604); and the hadith of Aisha with the editor's notes on its chains (6605-6606).
