Reports as transmitted, with no museum verdict. Source: Tafsir al-Tabari, Shakir ed., vol. 6, pp. 183-190.
- Passage 3:7 (volume 6, pp. 174-182, reports 6573-6590): al-Tabari's paraphrase of the verse's first half and the interpreters' five views on the firm and the resembling verses: the abrogating against the abrogated (Ibn Abbas through Ali ibn Abi Talha and Ibn Sa'd, al-Suddi, Qatada, al-Rabi', al-Dahhak, 6574-6584), the verses of lawful and unlawful against those that confirm one another (Mujahid, 6585-6586), the single-meaning against the multi-meaning (Muhammad ibn Jafar ibn al-Zubayr, 6587), the verses of the stories of the prophets against their repetitions across suras (Ibn Zayd, 6588), and the knowable against what God kept to Himself, with the separate letters and the Jewish attempt to calculate the term of Islam, Jabir ibn Abd Allah ibn Ri'ab's account and al-Tabari's reasoning (pp. 179-182); the editor's notes (the chain of 6573 and al-Hakim's Mustadrak, the Ibn Hisham text, al-Durr al-Manthur, the manuscript's slips and a sentence he suspects lost lines); and the start of the readers' views on the phrase for the foundation of the Book (6589-6590). Reports as transmitted, with no museum verdict. Source: Tafsir al-Tabari, Shakir ed., vol. 6, pp. 174-182.
- Passages 3:4 (close), 3:5, 3:6 and 3:7 (start) (volume 6, pp. 165-173, reports 6564-6572): al-Tabari's reading of the threat to those who denied the signs and of the two divine names that follow it (6564-6565, with the editor's note on the text lost after 6565); the verse that nothing escapes God's knowledge, read against the Najran Christians' account of Jesus (6566); the forming of humans in the womb, read as an argument about Jesus (Ibn Ishaq, al-Rabi') and as a description of creation in the womb (the report from Ibn Abbas, Ibn Mas'ud and Companions, with the editor's references to Muslim and al-Bukhari, and Qatada, 6567-6570); the two divine names closing 3:6 (6571-6572) with the editor's notes on the manuscript; and the opening of 3:7 in al-Tabari's reading: the Qur'an as the Book, the firm verses, why the word for 'mother' is singular, the Basran grammarian's view that it is a quotation and al-Tabari's rejection of it, the editor's notes on the poet and the manuscript, the debate over the word for 'others' and the start of the sense of 'resembling'. Reports as transmitted, with no museum verdict. Source: Tafsir al-Tabari, Shakir ed., vol. 6, pp. 165-173.
- Passages 3:2 (close), 3:3 and 3:4 (volume 6, pp. 156-164, reports 6548-6563): the rest of al-Tabari's account of the interpreters' views on the divine name of the Living (permanence and denial of death, ability to direct all that He wills, and the view that He has an everlasting life as He has knowledge and power), with his choice and his argument against the Najran Christians; the explanation of the Self-subsisting as the one who stands over everything in provision, protection and direction (Mujahid, al-Rabi') against the view of standing in place (Muhammad ibn Ja'far ibn al-Zubayr), al-Tabari's choice, his grammatical account of the three readings, the editor's notes (Sira of Ibn Hisham, the manuscript's slips, Farra') and the remark on Umar's preference; the sending down of the scripture with truth and its confirming of earlier books (6554-6558); the sending down of the Torah and the Gospel as clarification (6559-6560) and the editor's note on the manuscript word for 'your description'; the Furqan as separation in the matter of Jesus (Ibn Ishaq) or as the Qur'an and its rulings (Qatada, al-Rabi'), al-Tabari's choice and reasoning (6561-6563); and the start of his explanation of the threat to those who denied God's signs. Reports as transmitted, with no museum verdict. Source: Tafsir al-Tabari, Shakir ed., vol. 6, pp. 156-164.
- Passages 2:286 (close), 3:1 and 3:2 (volume 6, pp. 142-156, reports 6534-6549): the close of al-Tabari's commentary on al-Baqara (the disbelieving people, the word for protector, the reports that God answered each supplication, 6534-6542, with the editor's chain and variant notes, the report of Mu'adh, and the editor's note on the old manuscript's colophon), the start of Al Imran with al-Tabari's explanation of the opening, the account of the Najran Christian delegation (6543-6544) as transmitted from Ibn Ishaq and al-Rabi' with the editor's notes, the readers' difference over the word for the self-subsisting (6545-6547), and the first views on the word for the living (6548-6549), in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Tour stop for person/al-khwarizmi (`tour/halls.yaml`): synced its sources to the record (two sources, the Muqaddima of Ibn Khaldun and the Turkish algebra source, were added to the person record by the algebra invention and the validator requires the stop to match); no change in text.
- Passage 2:286 (volume 6, pp. 134-141, reports 6512-6533): the end of al-Tabari's two kinds of forgetting and his two kinds of error (with the editor's note on a line of poetry attributed to Ubayd ibn al-Abras, described and not reproduced), his answer to those who said the plea is only an act of humility, the supplication about the burden (the word for covenant, the views of covenant, transformation into apes and pigs, and weight, 6512-6524; the editor's note on Musa ibn Qays al-Hadrami), the supplication about what is beyond strength (6525-6530, with the editor's note on the chain of 6529), the supplications for pardon, forgiveness and mercy (6531-6533), and the start of the clause on the Lord as protector, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passages 2:285 (close) and 2:286 (start) (volume 6, pp. 126-133, reports 6500-6511): al-Tabari on the clause that the believers make no distinction between the messengers (the omitted word for 'saying', the reading with a letter ya and his choice of the nun reading by widespread transmission, 6500), on the believers' declaration of hearing, obeying and seeking forgiveness (the meaning of forgiveness, the grammar of the accusative with poetic illustration described, the report that Gabriel told the Prophet to ask, 6501), on the first clause of 2:286 (the meaning of the word for capacity, 6502-6504), on what a soul earns (6505-6508), on the supplication about forgetting and error (6509-6511) and his two kinds of forgetting, with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passages 2:284 (close) and 2:285 (start) (volume 6, pp. 118-125, reports 6496-6499): the rest of the editor's notes on report 6495 (the dispute over Ali ibn Zayd's grading, and the words explained), al-Tabari's own choice that the verse is firm and not abrogated with his reasons, the report of the believer's private reckoning and covering (6496-6497) with the editor's long chain notes, al-Tabari's answer to the objection from the verse on what a soul earns and his distinction between the doubter and the believer who only intended, the closing clause of 2:284, and the opening of 2:285 (the report that the Prophet said it was right for him to believe, the view that it came after the alarm of the Companions, and the readers' difference over the word for the books), in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passage 2:284 (volume 6, pp. 110-117, reports 6465-6495): the reports of the abrogation view (al-Shabi, Ibn Masud, Mujahid, al-Hasan, Qatada, Ibn Zayd, al-Suddi, Aisha), the view that the verse is firm and means God informs people of what they hid (Ibn Abbas, al-Rabi', al-Hasan, Mujahid), al-Tabari's explanation of the meanings on those reports, the view that the reckoning is by worldly griefs (Aisha, with the report on fever and misfortune), and the editor's notes on transmitters and sources, labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Library note for Tabari volume 6 (`docs/delegation/library/tafsir-tabari-shakir.md`): records the gap at printed p. 56 (reports on the meaning of the word for justice dropped by the copyist), the report-number jump 6275 to 6277, and the unclear note on p. 93.
- Passage 2:284 (volume 6, pp. 102-109, reports 6451-6464): the reports that the verse concerns the concealing of testimony (Ibn Abbas, Ikrima, al-Shabi), the view that it announced reckoning for what hearts held and was then lifted by 2:286, with the reports of Abu Hurayra, Ibn Abbas and Ibn Umar on the alarm of the Companions and Sa'id ibn Jubayr's mursal reports, and the editor's long notes on the transmitters, gradings and parallel sources (Ahmad, Muslim, Ibn Hibban, al-Hakim, Ibn Kathir, al-Suyuti), labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passages 2:282 (close), 2:283 and 2:284 (start) (volume 6, pp. 93-102, reports 6434-6448): the close of 2:282 (the command to take God as a shield, His teaching, His knowledge), all of 2:283 (the readings of the word for scribe, the plural of the word for pledge, the trust between creditor and debtor, the pledge in travel and residence and the report that the Prophet pledged his armour, the concealing of testimony), and al-Tabari's opening of 2:284 on God's ownership and the reckoning, with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict or legal ruling.
- Passage 2:282 (volume 6, pp. 86-92, reports 6414-6433): the rest of the reports on the clause forbidding harm to the scribe and witness (harm by refusing, and the reading in which the one who asks is forbidden to harm them, with the readings of Umar, Ibn Masud and Mujahid), al-Tabari's choice and three reasons for it, and the reports on the clause that doing so is wrongdoing (disobedience; lying), with the editor's repairs and notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict or legal ruling.
- Passage 2:282 (volume 6, pp. 78-86, reports 6399-6413): why writing is more upright for testimony and nearer to freedom from doubt, the exception for present trade with al-Tabari's reading and grammar (including the lines he cites, described, and the editor's identification of the poets as doubtful), the dispute whether witnessing at a sale is binding with the reports, al-Tabari's choice that it is, the editor's grading of al-Rabi' ibn Sabih, and the first reports on the clause forbidding harm to the scribe and the witness, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict or legal ruling.
- Passage 2:282 (volume 6, pp. 70-77, reports 6379-6398): the remaining reports on when the witnesses may not refuse (Mujahid, Abu Mijlaz, al-Sha'bi, Ikrima, Ata', al-Hasan, Said ibn Jubayr, al-Suddi, Ibn Zayd, al-Dahhak, Atiyya al-Awfi), al-Tabari's choice and his reasoning from the name 'witnesses', the clause on not tiring of writing small or large debts with the meaning of the verb and a description of the two poets' lines he cites, and the opening of the clause on justice and uprightness, with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict or legal ruling.
- Passage 2:282 (volume 6, pp. 64-70, reports 6361-6378): the rest of the readings on one woman erring and the other reminding (Ibn Uyayna's meaning, the reading of al-A'mash), al-Tabari's choice of reading and his reasons for rejecting Ibn Uyayna's meaning as reported, the reports in his favour, and the first three views on when the witnesses must not refuse when called (to attend, to give, or both), with the editor's grading of Abu Amir and other notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict or legal ruling.
- Passage 2:282 (volume 6, pp. 57-63, reports 6348-6360): who counts as the foolish, the weak and the one unable to dictate and who dictates for them (with al-Tabari's choice and his argument against taking the foolish as a child), the two witnesses and what free men from among you means, the options of one man and two women with al-Tabari's grammar, the approved witnesses, and the opening of the two readings of the clause on one woman erring and reminding the other, with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict or legal ruling.
- Passage 2:282 (volume 6, pp. 49-56, reports 6334-6347; also repairs a verse-wording fragment in the Arabic of report 6322): the rest of the abrogation reports on writing the debt (al-Sha'bi, Abu Sa'id al-Khudri), the clauses on the scribe writing fairly and not refusing, the reports on whether the scribe's duty is binding, al-Tabari's choice that the command to write is binding and his argument against abrogation (with the tayammum and zihar comparisons), the stated gap where the copyist dropped the reports on 'justice', and the debtor's dictating, with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict or legal ruling.
- Passages 2:281 close and 2:282 start (volume 6, pp. 40-49, reports 6313-6333): the further last-revealed reports (Atiya, al-Suddi, Ibn Abbas with Ibn Jurayj's note on the Prophet's last days, Ibn al-Musayyib on the verse of debt), al-Tabari's explanation of the day of return, his meaning of contracting debt to a fixed term (including loan and advance sale), the reports that the verse concerns advance sale, his answer on why 'with a debt' is added, and the opening of the dispute whether writing the debt is obligatory or was abrogated, with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict or legal ruling.
- Passages 2:280 close and 2:281 start (volume 6, pp. 30-40, reports 6281-6312): the reports that the verse concerns usury (Shurayh, Ibn Abbas, al-Dahhak, al-Suddi, Mujahid, Ibrahim), al-Tabari's choice and his argument that the deferral applies to every due debt of a debtor in hardship, the two views on the remitting clause with his choice, the reports that the usury verses and 2:281 were the last revealed, and the editor's gradings and documentation labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict or legal ruling.
- Passages 2:279 close and 2:280 start (volume 6, pp. 26-30, reports 6268-6280): capital but no surplus (Qatada, al-Dahhak, al-Suddi), the farewell sermon on pre-Islamic usury with the editor's documentation labelled as his, al-Tabari's explanation of the close of 2:279, his grammar and reading of the clause on hardship (including Ubayy's reading, which he declines), and the reports of Ibn Abbas, Shurayh, al-Nakha'i and al-Sha'bi given as transmitted, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict or legal ruling.
- Passages 2:278 close and 2:279 start (volume 6, pp. 23-26, reports 6260-6267): al-Dahhak on the remaining usury, the two readings of the warning of war, al-Tabari's choice and argument for the short-alif reading with his reasoning on who is notified, the transmitted reports of Ibn Abbas, Qatada and al-Rabi (given as sayings, not as a ruling), and the start of the clause on repentance, with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict or legal ruling.
- Passages 2:276 close, 2:277 and 2:278 start (volume 6, pp. 19-23, reports 6257-6259): the closing report on God accepting charity with the editor's quotation of al-Tirmidhi on the attributes, al-Tabari's explanations of the close of 2:276 and of 2:277, and the start of 2:278 with the occasion-of-revelation reports (al-Abbas and the Banu al-Mughira; Thaqif and Attab ibn Usayd), the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict or legal ruling.
- Passage 2:276 (volume 6, pp. 17-19, reports 6254-6256): the Abu Hurayra and A'isha reports on God accepting only good charity and nurturing it, transmitted as they came, with the editor's long documentation, narrator remarks and gradings (including his reply to Ibn Kathir) labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passages 2:275 close and 2:276 start (volume 6, pp. 14-16, reports 6250-6253): al-Tabari on the admonition and what is past, the blotting out of usury and the increase of charity with the reports of Ibn Mas'ud and Abu Hurayra, and the editor's grading and sources, including his note on an old error in the Musnad, labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passage 2:275 (volume 6, pp. 11-13, report 6249): the meaning of the devil's touch, al-Tabari's reply that the warning is not limited to eating, his account of the pre-Islamic claim that sale is like usury and of the two kinds of addition, the report on the curse on those involved with usury with the editor's sources, and the editor's personal remark on his own time labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict or legal ruling.
- Passage 2:275 (volume 6, pp. 7-10, reports 6235-6248): the start of the commentary on usury, al-Tabari's lexical account of riba, the pre-Islamic practice as reported by Mujahid and Qatada, and the reports on how those who took it are raised, with the editor's textual notes and narrator remarks labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict or legal ruling. Also corrects the library note: the volume 6 commentary ends on printed p. 592.
- Library note on al-Tabari (Shakir edition): volume 5 is now read to its end (2:274), and the offset check for volume 6 is recorded (offset zero; commentary on printed pp. 7-592, 2:275 to 3:92; indexes from marker 594).
- Passages 2:273 close and 2:274 (pp. 600-602, reports 6229-6234; end of volume 5): reports on insistent asking and the Prophet's sayings via Qatada, the editor's note on a gap in the text labelled as his, Abu al-Darda on spending on horses, Qatada on spending without waste, and Ibn Abbas on these verses preceding the zakat details of Bara'a, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict or legal ruling.
- Passage 2:273 (pp. 596-599, reports 6227-6228): al-Tabari's choice that the mark is perceived by sight, his answers on the denial of insistent asking, and the Abu Sa'id al-Khudri report on abstaining with the editor's grading and sources labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passage 2:273 (pp. 593-596, reports 6221-6226): the meaning of abstaining and the mark by which the poor are known (humility; the mark of poverty; shabby clothes), al-Tabari's lexical notes and the editor's long notes on Ibn Anqa' al-Fazari labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passage 2:273 (pp. 591-593, reports 6212-6220): the poor held back in God's way as the Emigrants, al-Tabari's reading of being held back with his linguistic reply to al-Suddi's view that the polytheists confined them, and reports on being unable to travel for trade, with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passages 2:272 close and 2:273 start (pp. 587-590, reports 6203-6211): the occasion-of-revelation reports on charity to polytheist relatives (Sa'id ibn Jubayr, Ibn Abbas, Qatada, al-Rabi, al-Suddi), the editor's supplied text for a gap in 6209 labelled as his, and the start of 2:273 on the poor held back in God's way, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.

- Passage 2:259 (pp. 464-467, reports 5923-5932): further glosses on the word for not changing (al-Suddi, al-Dahhak, Ibn Abbas, Ikrima, Ibn Zayd, Bakr ibn Mudar) and the view that it means not rotting (Mujahid), al-Tabari's argument against deriving it from the muddy-clay or stale-water roots (labelled as his), and the opening of his comment on the command to look at the donkey, with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passage 2:259 (pp. 459-464, reports 5918-5923): the food and drink, the two readings of the word on not changing, al-Tabari's choice of the reading that keeps the final letter (labelled as his) with his arguments, the reports on Uthman, Zayd ibn Thabit and Ubayy ibn Ka'b and the codices, and the first glosses (Wahb, Qatada, al-Suddi), with the editor's notes labelled as his and a note that p. 464 is damaged in the scan, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passage 2:259 (pp. 454-459, reports 5911-5917): Wahb ibn Munabbih's further accounts (the Persian king's rebuilding, the restoration of Urmiya's body), al-Suddi on Uzayr, al-Tabari's explanation of the question about how long he stayed and the reply (labelled as his), and Qatada, al-Rabi' and Ibn Jurayj, with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; Israelite-source reports given as transmitted without a museum verdict.
- Passage 2:259 (pp. 447-454, report 5910): the long report from Wahb ibn Munabbih (via Ibn Humayd, Salama and Ibn Ishaq) on Urmiya, the fall of Jerusalem to Nebuchadnezzar and the century of death, summarised as transmitted from Israelite sources without a museum verdict, with the editor's comparison notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu.

- Two long-held figures join the People wing by the founder's rulings. Muhammad Ali Jinnah, the founder of Pakistan, finally has his page: his religious affiliation was argued in print and litigated in court for forty years and he never declared one himself, so the page records it as unknown with every reading preserved in full. Abdul Rahim Hatif, the Afghan vice president who served as acting president for two weeks in April 1992, is built under his own name and dates — the queue had carried a man who never existed in his place. Ahmed al-Hiba's page now also preserves, as a reading the founder considered and declined, the three further texts that attest his Sufi shaykhhood.

- The pathway total is now 300: the duplicate Alexandria trail (exhibit/path-alexandria-city) became an alias of exhibit/path-alexandria, and the homepage and learning pages say 300 in Arabic, English and Urdu.

- The People wing's two longest-open questions are closed on the evidence. Sharif Finch's page keeps his affiliation as unknown, and now records why the question is settled rather than pending: a commissioned evidence review found that the profile said to document Christian practice in his own words does not hold up — his only first-person statement calls himself religious and names no faith, and no text attests Islam for him. The same review confirms that four names once held back for a ruling stay out of the museum: Hasdai ibn Shaprut, Raja Todar Mal and Birbal, each attested in the affiliation the original rows gave them, and Tansen, whose affiliation the sources leave genuinely disputed and legendary.
- The Companions become a queryable set: the group record for the Sahaba is published, and the first 24 Companion records — al-Abbas, Ibn Abbas, Ibn Masud, Abu Bakr and their generation — now carry a sourced membership edge to it, the first batch of 474 under the coverage-expansion programme's opening phase, so the founding generation can be asked for as a set, not found name by name.

- Passage 2:251 (pp. 363-369, reports 5744-5746): the further accounts of David and Goliath from al-Suddi, al-Rabi' and Ibn Zayd (the horn, the mail, the stones, Talut's envy and the cave), with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passage 2:251 (pp. 357-363, reports 5741-5743): the three accounts of David and Goliath transmitted from Wahb b. Munabbih (two) and Mujahid, with the editor's notes labelled as his and the report that the People of the Book relate, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passages 2:249 close (pp. 352-353), 2:250 (pp. 353-354) and 2:251 start (pp. 354-357, report 5740): the grammar of the word for a band, the prayer for patience and steadfast feet, and the account from Wahb b. Munabbih of David and Goliath and Talut's turn against him, with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passage 2:249 (pp. 347-352, reports 5726-5739): the number who crossed the river and the Badr comparison, the views on who spoke the two sayings with al-Tabari's choice and argument (labelled as his), the sense of certainty of meeting God, with the editor's notes and the hadith references labelled as his and the scholars', in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passage 2:249 (pp. 340-346, reports 5710-5725): where the river was, the test of drinking and the handful (al-Tabari's reading and his choice of the reading of the word for the handful labelled as his), the reports on how many crossed, with the editor's notes and the hadith references labelled as his and the scholars', in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passages 2:248 close (pp. 335-338, reports 5701-5706) and 2:249 start (pp. 338-339, reports 5707-5709): how the angels bore the Ark with al-Tabari's choice (labelled as his), the closing clause read as a sign for believers, Talut's departure with the troops and the test by a river, in Arabic, English and Urdu; the library note now also records the skipped report number 5676 in volume 5.
- Passage 2:248 (pp. 328-334, reports 5680-5700): the further views on the meaning of the sakina with al-Tabari's choice of Ata's view (labelled as his), and the ten views on the remnant left by the house of Moses and the house of Aaron with his statement that none can be preferred without a report, with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passage 2:248 (pp. 322-328, reports 5661-5679): the broken report of Wahb b. Munabbih with the editor's note on the gap and the copy's colophon, the two views on where the Ark was with al-Tabari's choice (labelled as his), and the five views reported on the meaning of the sakina (Ali, Mujahid, Wahb, Ibn Abbas, al-Suddi), in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passage 2:248 (pp. 316-322, reports 5658-5660): the sign of the Ark as al-Tabari reads it, the reports of Wahb b. Munabbih on Eli, the capture and return of the Ark, and Ibn Abbas on the tablets and the Amalekites, with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passages 2:247 close (pp. 310-315, reports 5644-5657) and 2:248 start (pp. 315-316): the reports on the two tribes, the sense of the choosing and the added knowledge and stature, the verse that kingship is God's to give (al-Tabari's reading labelled as his), the gap noted by the editor, and al-Tabari's reading of the Ark story as a lesson, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passages 2:246 close (pp. 304-306) and 2:247 start (pp. 306-310, reports 5636-5643): the turning away of all but a few and al-Tabari's reading of it as a rebuke (labelled as his), then the objection to Talut (Saul) and the reports of Wahb b. Munabbih, al-Suddi, Ikrima, Qatada and al-Dahhak on the oil, the staff and the tribes, with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passage 2:246 (pp. 298-303, report 5635 and the grammar): al-Suddi's account of Sham'un's birth and call, al-Tabari's choice on the reading of the fighting verb, the prophet's question and the notables' answer, and al-Tabari's treatment of the grammar of the particle with the views of the grammarians and the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passage 2:246 (pp. 292-298, reports 5628-5634): who the prophet was (Sham'un, Shamwil or Joshua), why the notables asked for a king, the long account from Wahb b. Munabbih and the reports of al-Rabi', Ibn Abbas and al-Dahhak, with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passages 2:245 close (pp. 286-291, reports 5622-5625) and 2:246 start (pp. 291-292, reports 5626-5627): the readings of the multiplying verb with al-Tabari's choice (labelled as his), the withholding and expanding with the price report and the editor's and scholars' gradings attributed, the return, and the prophet of the Children of Israel named Shamwil with his lineage as transmitted, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passages 2:244-2:245 (pp. 280-286, reports 5617-5621): the command to fight and why al-Tabari rejects reading it as addressed to the revived people (labelled as his), the meaning of the loan to God and the reports on Abu al-Dahdah, with the editor's notes and chain judgements attributed to him and the scholars he cites, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:243 close (pp. 274-279, reports 5611-5616): the further reports on the people who left their homes, al-Tabari's choice on the sense of the plural for thousands and on their number (labelled as his), the lesson of the account and the note on the end of the old division, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:243 (pp. 267-274, reports 5598-5610): the reports on the people who left their homes, their number, the plague, Ezekiel and their return to life, from Wahb b. Munabbih, Ibn Abbas, al-Suddi, Muhammad b. Ishaq, Ibn Zayd and al-Hasan, with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu; reported without a museum verdict.
- Passages 2:241-2:243 (pp. 262-267, reports 5590-5597): who the divorced women's provision covers (Ata', Mujahid, Said b. Jubayr, Ibn Zayd) with al-Tabari's choice labelled as his, the clarity clause, and the opening of the account of those who left their homes (Ibn Abbas), with the editor's notes and the graders' judgements attributed, in Arabic, English and Urdu; stated as reports, not as a ruling.
- Passage 2:240 close (pp. 255-262, reports 5574-5589): the abrogation reports, the reports that it was by bequest, those that the ruling stands, al-Tabari's choice (labelled as his), the Furay'a report with the editor's notes and gradings labelled as his, the grammar and the leaving clause, in Arabic, English and Urdu; stated as reports, not as a ruling.
- Passage 2:239 close (pp. 248-250, reports 5570-5571) and 2:240 start (pp. 250-254, reports 5572-5573): the meaning of being safe after fear (Mujahid and Ibn Zayd) with al-Tabari's choice, then the 2:240 widow's year, the two readings of the word for the bequest and al-Tabari's choice of the nominative with his arguments (labelled as his), the abrogation reports of Qatada and al-Rabi', with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:239 (pp. 241-247, reports 5547-5569): the reports on praying in fear on foot or mounted (Qatada, al-Zuhri, al-Nakha'i, al-Hasan, Harim b. Hayyan, 'Ata', Jabir, Malik), al-Tabari's account of what counts as fear and of the number of units (labelled as his, other views under their names, no museum verdict and no ruling), and Ibn 'Umar's reports of the fear prayer, with the editor's gradings labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:238 close (pp. 235-237, reports 5529-5534) and 2:239 start (pp. 237-240, reports 5535-5546): the last views on the meaning of devotion and al-Tabari's own choice of obedience (labelled as his, other views kept under their names), then 2:239 on praying on foot or mounted in fear, the grammar and readings and the reports of al-Nakha'i, Sa'id b. Jubayr, al-Hasan, Mujahid, al-Dahhak and al-Suddi, with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:238 (pp. 228-234, reports 5498-5528): the three views on the meaning of devotion in the last clause (obedience, silence, stillness and humility) with the reports of Ibn 'Abbas, Mujahid, Ibn Mas'ud and Zayd b. Arqam, and the editor's gradings labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:238 (pp. 221-228, reports 5493-5497): al-Tabari's evidence for the afternoon prayer from sayings of the Prophet (Abu Basra, Buraydah and others), his own labelled surmise on why God urged guarding it, and his account of the name of the middle prayer, with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:238 (pp. 214-221, reports 5472-5492): the dawn-prayer view of Ibn 'Abbas, Abu al-'Aliya, Jabir, 'Ata', 'Ikrima, Mujahid and others with its ground, the view that it is one of the five and not known, and al-Tabari's own choice of the afternoon prayer (labelled as his, other views kept under their names, no museum verdict), with the editor's remarks labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:238 (pp. 208-214, reports 5461-5471): the reports of the reading with a conjunction and the afternoon prayer named after the middle one (Hafsa, 'A'isha, Ibn 'Abbas, 'Ubayd b. 'Umayr), described as a reading attributed to codices and not as Qur'an, the sunset-prayer view of Qubaysa b. Dhu'ayb, and the start of the dawn-prayer view, with the editor's remarks labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:238 (pp. 202-207, reports 5453-5460): the rest of the noon-prayer view (Zayd b. Thabit, Ibn 'Umar, Abu Sa'id al-Khudri, the Hafsa codex report, Usama b. Zayd), its grounds, and the start of the group who read the verse with an added word, with the editor's remarks labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:238 (pp. 196-202, reports 5441-5452): the last reports for the afternoon-prayer view and the start of the noon-prayer view (Zayd b. Thabit, Ibn 'Umar, Abu Sa'id al-Khudri), with the editor's gradings and his rejection of a raised form labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:238 (pp. 189-195, reports 5432-5440): more reports on the afternoon-prayer view (Abu Hurayra, Ibn 'Abbas, the Abu Hashim b. 'Utba report, Samura, Umm Habiba) and the report of al-Bara' b. 'Azib on a wording said to have been abrogated, given as transmitted with no verdict, with the editor's gradings labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:238 (pp. 182-188, reports 5419-5431): al-Tabari's grounds for the afternoon-prayer view, the reports of the Battle of the Trench from Ibn Mas'ud, 'Ali and others, with the editor's sources and gradings labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:238 (pp. 175-181, reports 5398-5418): more reports that the middle prayer is the afternoon prayer (Umm Salama, 'A'isha, Hafsa, Ibn 'Abbas, Qatada, al-Dahhak, Samura from the Prophet, Abu Ayyub), with the editor's gradings labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:238 (pp. 169-175, reports 5381-5397): the reports that the middle prayer is the afternoon prayer, from Ibn 'Abbas, 'Ali, Abu Hurayra, Ibn 'Umar, Abu Sa'id al-Khudri and 'A'isha, including the reports of an explanatory addition in 'A'isha's codex (described as reported, not as Qur'an), with the editor's gradings labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:237 close (pp. 164-167) and 2:238 start (pp. 167-168, reports 5364-5380): how forgiving brings one near to piety, the counsel of generosity between spouses with the reports of Jubayr b. Mut'im, Mujahid and others, the closing statement on God's seeing, then the start of 2:238 with Masruq on guarding the prayers and the first view of the middle prayer, the editor's grading labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:237 (pp. 157-163, reports 5353-5363): the last reports that the holder of the contract is the husband, al-Tabari's own choice of that view with his arguments from agreed rulings and the dialogue test of the guardian view (labelled as his, the opposing views kept under their names), and the three readings of who is addressed by the closing clause on forgiveness and piety, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:237 (pp. 150-156, reports 5307-5352): the rest of the view that the holder of the marriage contract is the guardian (Zayd b. Aslam, Rabi'a, Malik, Ibn Shihab, 'Ikrima), and the view that it is the husband ('Ali, Ibn 'Abbas, Jubayr b. Mut'im, Shurayh, Sa'id b. Jubayr and others), with the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:237 (pp. 142-149, reports 5251-5306): the reports on the forgiveness of the half dower by the woman herself (Ikrima, Mujahid, Shurayh, Ibn Shihab and others), and the first view on who holds the marriage tie, the guardian of the virgin, with Shurayh's change of view, the editor's notes labelled as his, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:236 close (pp. 136-140) and 2:237 start (pp. 140-142, reports 5244-5250): al-Tabari on the well-off and the straitened and the two readings of the measure, the grammar of the gift, the reports on men who marry and divorce quickly with the editor's gradings (labelled as his), other views of the lifted blame, and the 2:237 half-dower ruling, the forgiveness exception and the reports of Ibn 'Abbas, Mujahid, Qatada and al-Rabi', in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:236 (pp. 128-135, reports 5229-5243): the recommendation-only view of the divorce gift (Shurayh and others), the grounds of those who exempted a woman with a fixed dower, al-Tabari's own choice and long argument that the gift is a binding right of every divorced woman (labelled as his, with the opposing views kept under their names and no museum verdict), the editor's reconstruction of a corrupt passage, and the reports on the unanimous case of the woman without a fixed dower, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:236 (pp. 122-128, reports 5198-5228): the amounts of the divorce gift (Shurayh, al-Sha'bi, Ibn Sirin, Ibn Shihab), the Hanafi half-dower view as theirs, al-Tabari's own choice that it follows the husband's means, and the scholars' positions on whether the gift is obligatory for every divorced woman, all but one with a fixed dower, or partly by conscience (Hasan, Ibn 'Umar, Sa'id ibn al-Musayyab, al-Zuhri), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passages 2:235 (close, pp. 115-117, reports 5181-5189) and 2:236 first touch (pp. 117-121, reports 5190-5197): the end of the waiting-period ban and the closing sentence on God's knowledge, forgiveness and forbearance; on 2:236 the figure of 'touching', the two readings and al-Tabari's view that both are sound, who the verse covers, the meaning of fixing a portion, and the reports on the divorce gift (Ibn 'Abbas, al-Sha'bi), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:235 (pp. 109-115, reports 5162-5180): al-Tabari's choice that the forbidden 'secret' is intercourse, with his arguments against the pledge reading, the exception for fair speech and the reports on it, and the opening of the ban on resolving on the marriage contract during the waiting period, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:235 (pp. 102-108, reports 5126-5161): al-Tabari on the words for proposal and allusion, hiding the wish in the heart, his argument from the difference between allusion and plain statement (with the accusation question left unsettled), and the two readings of the forbidden secret promise (fornication, or taking a pledge in the waiting period), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:235 first touch (pp. 95-101, reports 5098-5125): what the early authorities allowed as a hint of marriage to a woman in her waiting period (Ibn 'Abbas, Mujahid, Sa'id ibn Jubayr, al-Qasim ibn Muhammad, 'Ata', Ibrahim, al-Sha'bi), the report of Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali and Sakina bint Hanzala, and the grammarians' views on the word for proposal with al-Tabari's own, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:234 (close, pp. 88-94, reports 5090-5097): the argument of those who make mourning and staying in the house obligatory (al-Fari'a hadith) as al-Tabari gives it, his grammar of the number ten and its nights, the reports on why ten days are added, and his reading of the clauses on the guardians and on God's awareness, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:234 (pp. 82-88, reports 5075-5089): the Umm Salama and Umm Habiba hadith on mourning and the Jahiliyya custom, A'isha's and Ibn 'Umar's rulings on what a widow avoids, and the view that the waiting concerns only husbands (al-Hasan, Ibn 'Abbas, the Asma' report), with the editor's tracing and grading, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passages 2:233 (close, pp. 75-76) and 2:234 first touch (pp. 77-81, reports 5071-5074): al-Tabari's reasons for his choice on the nursing-clause and his gloss on the closing sentence; on 2:234 his treatment of the missing predicate, the meaning of the widow's waiting period, and the reports and hadiths (Ibn 'Abbas, Ibn Shihab, Umm Salama, Hafsa), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:233 (pp. 68-74, reports 5049-5070): the positions on the time of weaning and al-Tabari's choice, and the clause on hiring wet nurses with the interpreters' readings of handing over what was given and his choice, in Arabic, English and Urdu; a leftover verse-adjacent phrase in the Arabic of an earlier batch is reworded.
- Passage 2:233 (pp. 62-68, reports 5029-5048): the last reports on the heir clause, al-Tabari's own choice and his argument (attributed as his, with other scholars' positions left unjudged), and the opening of the weaning clause with the first reports on the time of weaning, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:233 (pp. 57-62, reports 4997-5028): the remaining positions on who the heir is (guardian, the child's heirs, the forbidden-degree kin, the infant himself, the surviving parent) and on what the like duty covers (nursing wage and upkeep), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:233 (pp. 50-56, reports 4984-4996): the last reports on harming a mother or father through the child, al-Tabari's grammar answers and his view of the leader's duty, and the first positions on who the heir is and what he owes, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:233 (pp. 45-50, reports 4973-4983): al-Tabari on the sentence limiting what a soul is charged with and his reply to the qadar reading, the readers' difference on the ban on harming a mother or father through the child, his choice, and the interpreters' reports, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:233 (pp. 38-44, reports 4968-4972): al-Tabari's own choice on the limit of nursing, his answers to the objection from the thirty-month verse, the readers' difference on the clause on completing nursing, and the father's duty of provision and clothing, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:233 (pp. 32-38, reports 4950-4967): al-Tabari on why the verse adds completeness to the two years, and the interpreters' four positions on the limit of nursing (some infants only, infants whose parents differ, no nursing after two years, a duty on mothers eased afterwards), with the editor's notes on narrators, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passages 2:232 (rest, pp. 27-31) and 2:233 first touch (pp. 30-32, reports 4948-4949): al-Tabari's argument on the guardian (attributed as his), the demonstrative, purity and the knowledge of hearts, and the opening of 2:233 on mothers and nursing, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:232 (pp. 20-26, reports 4932-4947): the remaining occasion reports (Ma'qil ibn Yasar and Jabir ibn 'Abd Allah), the reports that read it as a ban on guardians' obstruction, al-Tabari's own choice, his word study of the verb, the report on marriage gifts, and the start of his argument on the guardian (his view, attributed as his), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passages 2:231 (rest, pp. 13-16, reports 4924-4926) and 2:232 first touch (pp. 17-19, reports 4927-4931, the Ma'qil ibn Yasar reports): al-Tabari's reading of the clauses on God's favour, the Book, wisdom and admonition, and the occasion reports with the editor's notes, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:231 first touch (vol. 5, pp. 7-13): al-Tabari's explanation of keeping and releasing divorced women at the end of their term, the reports on harmful repeated taking-back, the root of the word for release, and the clauses on wronging oneself and on mockery of God's signs, in Arabic, English and Urdu; the library note now records the volume 5 page offset (zero) and a gap of printed pp. 423-426 in the scan.
- Passage 2:230 closes (vol. 4, pp. 595-600): the last hadith on the second husband, the clause on the two returning to each other, the meaning of hoping to keep God's limits, and the closing sentence of the verse, in Arabic, English and Urdu; the library note records that volume 4 is finished and that volume 5 is not in the mounted harvest folder, so its offset is still to be set.
- Passage 2:230 continues (vol. 4, pp. 589-595): the reports from the Prophet on the second husband's consummation, from 'A'isha (the wife of Rifa'a), Abu Hurayra, Anas and Ibn 'Abbas, with the editor's notes on their chains, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:229 closes (vol. 4, pp. 583-585): the meaning of God's limits; passage 2:230 first touch (pp. 585-589): the views on the clause on a woman not being lawful to her divorcer until another husband, al-Tabari's choice of Mujahid's reading, his reasoning that both contract and consummation are meant, and the hadith of 'A'isha, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:229 continues (vol. 4, pp. 577-583): more reports on the amount of the ransom (Umm al-Rubayyi' and 'Uthman, Ibn 'Abbas, Qabisa), the claim by Bakr ibn 'Abdallah that it is abrogated by the verse in al-Nisa', and al-Tabari's choice and his two reasons against abrogation, then the start of the clause on God's limits, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:229 continues (vol. 4, pp. 571-577): al-Tabari's refusal of the grammarian's two aspects, and the views on whether the ransom is limited to the dower or may be any amount, with the reports of 'Umar, Ibn 'Umar, al-Hasan and others, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:229: replaced one more short typed verse phrase in the Arabic Tabari prose with a paraphrase.
- Passage 2:229: replaced short typed phrases from the verse in the Tabari prose with paraphrases, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:229 continues (vol. 4, pp. 565-571): al-Tabari on why blame is lifted from both spouses for the ransom, his question on the woman's own sin in paying, the hadith that call women who seek separation without cause hypocrites with the editor's gradings of their chains, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:229 continues (vol. 4, pp. 559-565): the remaining views on when taking a ransom is lawful, al-Tabari's choice that both must fear falling short and his answer to the objection, and the views on what the limits of God are, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:229 continues (vol. 4, pp. 553-559): the reports on the wives of Thabit ibn Qays (Habiba and Jamila) and the editor's note that they were two women, and the first views on what the fear of not keeping God's limits means, with the graded steps in the reports of Sa'id ibn Jubayr and al-Dahhak, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:229 continues (vol. 4, pp. 547-553): the meaning of keeping and releasing, the prohibition on taking back the dower, the variant readings of the verb for fearing and al-Tabari's choice among them, the state in which taking a payment is allowed, and the report of the first khul' (Jamila and Thabit ibn Qays), in Arabic, English and Urdu; also paraphrases typed verse phrases in earlier Arabic prose.
- Passage 2:229 continues (vol. 4, pp. 541-547): the views on the verse as a number of divorces or as the sunna of divorce, al-Tabari's choice of the number reading, the meaning of keeping or releasing, the Abu Razin report and the editor's objection to it, in Arabic, English and Urdu; also rewords a close paraphrase of the 2:228 wording in the Arabic prose.
- Passage 2:229: removed a short typed phrase from the verse in the Tabari prose, keeping a paraphrase, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:228 closes (vol. 4, pp. 535-538): al-Tabari's reasoning for the degree men have, and the meaning of the closing words of the verse; passage 2:229 first touch (pp. 538-541): the view that the verse sets the number of divorces, with the reports of 'Urwa, Qatada and Ibn Zayd and the editor's notes on the hadith chains, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:228 continues (vol. 4, pp. 529-535): the ruling on a husband who takes his wife back intending harm, the clause on women's rights like those over them, al-Tabari's choice, and the views on the degree men have over women, with his choice of Ibn 'Abbas's reading, in Arabic, English and Urdu; also replaces a typed verse title phrase from the previous batch with a link to 2:229.
- Passage 2:228 continues (vol. 4, pp. 524-529): al-Tabari's reasoning that the prohibition covers both menses and pregnancy, his answer on the mention of belief, the meaning and plurals of the word for husbands, and the reports that the husband has more right to take her back during the waiting period, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:228 continues (vol. 4, pp. 518-523): the views on what the divorced woman may not hide, namely menses, pregnancy or both, the reasons given for the prohibition, and al-Tabari's own choice of menses and pregnancy, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:228: replaced three short typed Qur'an phrases (from 65:1 and 2:228) in the Tabari prose with links to the verses, in Arabic, English and Urdu, and re-ran the verse-text sweep over the vol. 4 files (0 pending).
- Passage 2:228 continues (vol. 4, pp. 512-517): al-Tabari's reasoning that the word for the waiting period can mean either the time of menses or of purity, his own choice of three periods of purity, his reply to the objection about the second purity and to the view on the four-month oath, his explanation of the word for divorced women, and the first reports on what God created in their wombs, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:228 continues (vol. 4, pp. 506-511): the view that the waiting period means purity between menses, with the reports of 'A'isha, Zayd ibn Thabit and Ibn 'Umar, the case of al-Ahwas referred to Mu'awiya, and the opening of al-Tabari's discussion of the word in Arab usage, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:228 continues (vol. 4, pp. 500-505): the view that the word for the waiting period means menstruation, with the reports of Mujahid, Ibn 'Abbas, 'Ikrima and Qatada, and the rulings of 'Umar, Ibn Mas'ud, Abu Musa, 'Ali and 'Uthman that the husband may take her back until she bathes after the third menses, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:227 closes (vol. 4, pp. 493-499: the view that the husband is made to stand, the view that swearing off is nothing, and al-Tabari's own choice with his reasoning from the verse's ending) and 2:228 gets its first touch (p. 499), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:227 continues (vol. 4, pp. 487-493): the view that the husband is made to stand after four months and must return or divorce, with the reports of 'Umar, 'Ali, 'Uthman, Abu al-Darda', 'A'isha, Ibn 'Umar and twelve Companions, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:227 continues (vol. 4, pp. 481-487): the reports that the passing of four months brings a final single divorce (Ibn Mas'ud, Ibn 'Abbas, 'Umar, 'Ali, Shurayh, Masruq, al-Hasan, al-Nakha'i and others) and the opposing reports that it is a divorce the husband may revoke (Sa'id ibn al-Musayyab, Abu Bakr ibn 'Abd al-Rahman, Makhul, Rabi'a), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:226 closes (vol. 4, pp. 475-477: the views on expiation when the husband returns, with al-Tabari's choice) and 2:227 gets its first touch (pp. 477-481: whether the passing of four months itself divorces, with the reports of 'Ali, Ibn Mas'ud, 'Uthman, Zayd ibn Thabit and Ibn 'Abbas), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:226 continues (vol. 4, pp. 469-475): the reports on returning where there is an excuse, al-Tabari's own choice that returning means intercourse (with the resolve of the heart sufficing while an excuse lasts), and the views on the closing clause of forgiveness and mercy, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:226 continues (vol. 4, pp. 464-469): the reasoning behind each view on which oath amounts to swearing off, al-Tabari's own choice, the meaning of the word for returning with the reports on what counts as returning (intercourse, or a declaration where there is an excuse), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Added a list of the Arabic and Urdu commentary spans in 2:1 to 2:195 that match the Qur'an text only approximately and were deliberately left unchanged, so a later pass can resolve them.
- Verse-text repair, sweep batch 15: Arabic and Urdu commentary files had typed verse wording replaced by links to the verse in the frozen Tanzil text (exact matches only).
- Passage 2:226 continues (vol. 4, pp. 457-463): the reports on what kind of oath amounts to swearing off from a wife (anger and intent to harm, any oath barring intercourse, any oath to her harm), from 'Ali, Ibn 'Abbas, al-Hasan, al-Nakha'i, al-Sha'bi, al-Zuhri and Sa'id ibn al-Musayyab, with the editor's notes, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Verse-text repair, sweep batch 14: 25 Arabic and Urdu commentary files had typed verse wording replaced by links to the verse in the frozen Tanzil text (exact matches only).
- Passage 2:225 closes (vol. 4, pp. 451-455: the views on the clause about what hearts have earned, al-Tabari's own two-part choice, and the closing remark) and 2:226 gets its first touch (pp. 456-457: the meaning of swearing off and the first view on the kind of oath), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Verse-text repair, sweep batch 13: 25 Arabic and Urdu commentary files had typed verse wording replaced by links to the verse in the frozen Tanzil text (exact matches only).
- Verse-text repair, sweep batch 12: 25 Arabic and Urdu commentary files had typed verse wording replaced by links to the verse in the frozen Tanzil text (exact matches only).
- Passage 2:225 continues (vol. 4, pp. 446-451): al-Tabari's own choice on the meaning of idle talk in oaths (with his rejection of the view that it covers oaths to disobedience), and the start of his treatment of the clause on what hearts have earned, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Verse-text repair, sweep batch 11: 25 Arabic and Urdu commentary files had typed verse wording replaced by links to the verse in the frozen Tanzil text (exact matches only).
- Passage 2:225 continues (vol. 4, pp. 440-446): the last views on the idle-talk oaths (oaths to disobedience, filler oaths, self-imprecations, oaths broken in forgetfulness) with the editor's gradings, and the start of al-Tabari's own reasoning on the word for idle talk, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Verse-text repair, sweep batch 10: 25 Arabic and Urdu commentary files had typed verse wording replaced by links to the verse in the frozen Tanzil text (exact matches only).
- Passage 2:225 continues (vol. 4, pp. 434-440): the remaining views on the idle-talk oaths (oaths sworn in the belief they are true, oaths in anger, oaths to disobedience), with the editor's chain notes and corrections, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Verse-text repair, sweep batch 9: 25 Arabic and Urdu commentary files had typed verse wording replaced by links to the verse in the frozen Tanzil text (exact matches only).
- Passage 2:225 continues (vol. 4, pp. 429-434): the reports on the idle-talk oaths from 'A'isha, al-Sha'bi, Mujahid, Abu Hurayra, Ibn 'Abbas, al-Hasan and al-Nakha'i, in two groups (habitual phrases of speech, and oaths sworn in the belief they are true), with the editor's notes, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Verse-text repair, sweep batch 8: 25 Arabic and Urdu commentary files had typed verse wording replaced by links to the verse in the frozen Tanzil text (exact matches only).
- Passage 2:224 closes (vol. 4, pp. 424-427: al-Tabari's choice on the word for 'exposure', with the poets' usage and his rejection of al-Suddi's dating) and 2:225 gets its first touch (pp. 427-429: the idle-talk oaths, Ibn 'Abbas and 'A'isha), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Verse-text repair, sweep batch 7: 25 Arabic and Urdu commentary files had typed verse wording replaced by links to the verse in the frozen Tanzil text (exact matches only).
- Passage 2:223 closes (vol. 4, pp. 418-419) and 2:224 gets its first touch (pp. 419-423): the views on the prohibition of making God an object for oaths (Tawus, Ibn 'Abbas, Qatada, 'Ata', al-Dahhak, al-Suddi, al-Nakha'i, Mujahid, 'A'isha), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Verse-text repair, sweep batch 6: 25 Arabic and Urdu commentary files had typed verse wording replaced by links to the verse in the frozen Tanzil text (exact matches only).
- Passage 2:223 continues (vol. 4, pp. 414-418): the close of al-Tabari's own choice on the word 'anna' with the poets' usage, and his reading of the clause on making provision for oneself, with the competing view of 'Ata', in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Wording repair in the 2:223 commentary: the remaining quoted two-word verse clause in Arabic, English and Urdu was replaced by a plain reference to the word 'anna', in line with the no-typed-Qur'an rule.
- Verse-text repair, sweep batch 5: 25 Arabic and Urdu commentary files had typed verse wording replaced by links to the verse in the frozen Tanzil text (exact matches only).
- Passage 2:223 continues (vol. 4, pp. 410-414): the reports of Umm Salama, Jabir, 'Umar and the Himyar visitors on the occasion of the verse, and the start of al-Tabari's own choice on the meaning of the word for 'however', in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Verse-text repair, sweep batch 4: 25 Arabic and Urdu commentary files had typed verse wording replaced by links to the verse in the frozen Tanzil text (exact matches only).
- Passage 2:223 continues (vol. 4, pp. 405-410): the reports of Ibn 'Umar, Abu al-Darda', the withdrawal view, and the reports on the occasion of the verse (Mujahid from Ibn 'Abbas, Jabir, Umm Salama), with the editor's gradings and chain notes, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Verse-text repair, sweep batch 3: 25 Arabic and Urdu commentary files had typed verse wording replaced by links to the verse in the frozen Tanzil text; only spans that match Tanzil exactly were changed.
- Passage 2:223 continues (vol. 4, pp. 401-405): the rest of the views on the word for 'however' or 'whenever' (Ibn 'Abbas, Mujahid, 'Ata', al-Dahhak, Ibn 'Umar via Nafi', Malik) with the editor's notes on chains and textual corrections, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Verse-text repair, sweep batch 2: 25 Arabic and Urdu commentary files for 2:16 to 2:27 had typed verse wording replaced by links to the verse in the frozen Tanzil text, as the no-typed-Qur'an rule requires.
- Passage 2:222 closes (vol. 4, pp. 396-397) and 2:223 gets its first touch (pp. 397-401): the field metaphor, why women are called a tilth, and the views on the meaning of the word for 'however' (Ibn 'Abbas, 'Ikrima, Mujahid, Muhammad ibn Ka'b, Qatada, al-Suddi and the account of the Jewish man's remark), in Arabic, English and Urdu, without typed verse wording.
- Repair: swept al-Tabari commentary on 2:14 to 2:2x of volumes 1 to 3 (batch 1 of the sweep), replacing quoted verse phrases of three or more words in the Arabic and Urdu prose with passage links, matched against the frozen Tanzil text. Single words, two-word glosses, names, hadith and poetry are untouched.
- Repair: checked the passage links added in the earlier typed-verse repair for al-Tabari vol. 4 (2:196-2:221) against the frozen Tanzil text and corrected the ones pointing to a neighbouring or wrong verse (for example 2:200, 2:210 and 2:216), in the Arabic and Urdu prose.
- Passage 2:222: al-Tabari's commentary continues (vol. 4 pp. 391-396): the remaining reports on the time of cleanness, his argument against reading the command as permitting the place of the blood, and the views on who the purifiers are (those who wash for prayer, those who avoid the forbidden passage, those who do not return to sins) with his choice, in Arabic, English and Urdu, without typed verse wording.
- Passage 2:222: al-Tabari's commentary continues (vol. 4 pp. 386-391): his choice that being clean means having washed, and the two views on the command to come to them from where God commanded (the permitted passage versus the time of cleanness), with the reports of Ibn 'Abbas, Mujahid, 'Ikrima, Qatada, Ibrahim and Abu Razin, in Arabic, English and Urdu, without typed verse wording.
- Passage 2:222: al-Tabari's commentary continues (vol. 4 pp. 381-386): the navel-to-knee view and the Prophet's practice (Maymuna, 'A'isha), the readings on being clean (light versus doubled) and his choice of the doubled reading meaning washing, and the first reports on the permission to approach again (Ibn 'Abbas, Mujahid, 'Ikrima, al-Hasan, Ibrahim), in Arabic, English and Urdu, without typed verse wording.
- Passage 2:222: al-Tabari's commentary continues (vol. 4 pp. 376-381): the reports on what a man must avoid of a menstruating woman (Nudba for the Prophet's practice, 'A'isha, Ibn 'Abbas, Umm Salama, al-Hasan, Mujahid, 'Ikrima, Shurayh), the grounds of those who limit the avoidance to the place of the blood, and the view of the navel-to-knee boundary, with the editor's gradings, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:221: al-Tabari's commentary closes (vol. 4 pp. 370-371): the readings on giving women in marriage, the guardian's right, and the call to the Fire versus the call to Paradise. Passage 2:222: first touch (pp. 372-375), the question on menstruation, why the Companions asked, harm as filth or blood, and the first views on what to avoid (Qatada, al-Rabi', Mujahid, al-Suddi, Ibn 'Abbas, Ibn Sirin), in Arabic, English and Urdu, without typed verse wording.
- Repair: removed typed verse wording from the al-Tabari vol. 4 commentary on 2:196 to 2:221 (Arabic and Urdu quoted verse phrases of two or more words, and three transliterated phrases in English), replacing each with a link to the passage (the AGENTS.md rule that Qur'an text is never typed). Single-word lexical glosses, names, hadith and poetry are untouched.
- Passage 2:221: al-Tabari's commentary continues (vol. 4 pp. 363-369): the reports that the ban covers Arab polytheist women only (Qatada, Sa'id ibn Jubayr) or all non-Muslim women ('Umar and Talha and Hudhayfa, via Shahr ibn Hawshab), al-Tabari's choice and his argument against abrogation, the counter-reports ('Umar via Zayd ibn Wahb and Shaqiq, Jabir), and the clauses on the believing slave woman and "even if she pleases you" (Ibn Rawaha's story), with the editor's gradings, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:220: al-Tabari's commentary closes (vol. 4 pp. 357-361): God knowing the corrupter from the reformer, the views on the meaning of "la-a'natakum" (constraint versus destruction), and "mighty, wise". Passage 2:221: first touch (pp. 362-363), the question whether the ban on polytheist women is general and abrogated for People of the Book women or specific to Arab polytheists (Ibn 'Abbas, 'Ikrima, al-Hasan, Mujahid, al-Rabi', Qatada), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:220: al-Tabari's commentary continues (vol. 4 pp. 351-357): the reports on the orphans' property (Ibn 'Abbas, al-Sha'bi, 'Ata', Mujahid, al-Suddi, al-Dahhak, 'A'isha), his explanation of the verse, and his grammatical discussion of why "ikhwanukum" is nominative, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:219: al-Tabari's commentary closes (vol. 4 pp. 346-349): his reasons that the verse on al-'afw is not abrogated, the nasb and raf' readings of al-'afw, and the reports on the clause on reflecting on this world and the hereafter. Passage 2:220: first touch (pp. 349-351), the reports on the orphans' property (Ibn 'Abbas, Ibn Abi Layla, Qatada, al-Rabi'), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:219: al-Tabari's commentary continues (vol. 4 pp. 340-345): his choice that al-'afw is the surplus beyond one's own and one's family's needs, the supporting reports (Abu Hurayra, Jabir, Ibn Mas'ud) with the editor's notes, his replies to the zakat and 'unnoticeable' views, and the dispute whether the verse is abrogated by zakat; also a note on the garbled printed text in report 4142, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:219: al-Tabari's commentary continues (vol. 4 pp. 334-339): the stages of the prohibition of wine (al-Suddi, Qatada, Mujahid, al-Rabi', Ibn Zayd) with the editor's notes, then the start of the phrase on spending the surplus (al-'afw) and the six views of the interpreters on its meaning (surplus, the unnoticeable, the middle way, whatever they bring, the good), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:219: al-Tabari's commentary continues (vol. 4 pp. 329-334): his choice of the reading kabir, the meaning of "their sin is greater than their benefit" (before versus after prohibition), and the reports on the stages of the prohibition of wine (Sa'id ibn Jubayr, Ibn 'Umar, 'Ikrima, al-Hasan, Abu al-Qumus, al-Sha'bi), with the editor's gradings and notes, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:219: al-Tabari's commentary continues (vol. 4 pp. 323-328): the views on maysir as all gambling (Ibn Sirin, al-Hasan, Tawus, 'Ata', Ibn Mas'ud, al-Qasim, Ibn 'Abbas, Mujahid and others), the "great sin" of wine and gambling and his choice, the benefits before prohibition, and the two readings kabir and kathir, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passages 2:218 and 2:219: al-Tabari's commentary covers 2:218 (vol. 4 pp. 317-320: the emigrants who strove in God's way, the roots of hijra and jihad, and the reports tying it to the party of 'Abdallah ibn Jahsh) and begins 2:219 (pp. 320-322: the roots of khamr and maysir and Mujahid and Ibn Mas'ud on gambling), with the source registered on both, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:217: al-Tabari's commentary closes (vol. 4 pp. 311-317): his refutation of al-Farra's two analyses of the raising of "sadd", the views on whether the verse on fighting in the sacred month is abrogated (Ibn Maysara, al-Zuhri, 'Ata') and his choice with the evidence of Hunayn, Ta'if and Hudaybiya, "they will not cease to fight you", and the verse on apostasy, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:217: al-Tabari's commentary continues (vol. 4 pp. 306-311): the further reports on the raid at Nakhla (Jundub, Mujahid, al-Zuhri and Miqsam, Ibn 'Abbas, Abu Malik, Qatada, Ibn Jurayj, al-Dahhak, al-Sha'bi), the editor's grading of the mursal report and identification of Miqsam, and al-Tabari's use of the Mujahid and al-Dahhak reports on the grammar of "sadd", in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:217: al-Tabari's commentary continues (vol. 4 pp. 300-306): the reading of "qital", why the Arabs honoured Rajab, the grammar of the verse and his rejection of al-Farra's joining of "the Sacred Mosque" to "fighting", and the account of the raid of 'Abdallah ibn Jahsh at Nakhla and the killing of Ibn al-Hadrami as the occasion of revelation, from Ibn Ishaq via 'Urwa and from al-Suddi, with the editor's notes on the date variants, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passages 2:215-2:217: al-Tabari's commentary closes 2:215 (vol. 4 pp. 292-295: the second analysis of "madha" and whether the verse concerned voluntary spending before zakat), covers 2:216 (pp. 295-299: the prescription of fighting and the views on who it binds, his rejection of the claimed abrogation by 2:285 and his preference for the communal obligation, kurh/karh, and the editor's grading of the Ibn 'Abbas report) and begins 2:217 (p. 299), source registered on 2:216 and 2:217, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passages 2:213-2:215: al-Tabari's commentary closes 2:213 (vol. 4 pp. 286-287: "by His leave", the question whether they were guided to the truth or the difference, and his Arabic parallels), covers 2:214 (pp. 287-291: the Trench context, "wa-lamma", and the raised or subjunctive reading of "hatta yaqula" with his choice) and begins 2:215 (pp. 291-292: what to spend and on whom, and the two analyses of "madha"), source registered on 2:214 and 2:215, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:213: al-Tabari's commentary continues (vol. 4 pp. 279-285): his reasoning that the "one community" was on faith, the meaning of baghy and the grammarians' dispute over "min", the Friday report of Abu Hurayra with the editor's grading, Ibn Zayd's list of what they differed over, and the reports of al-Rabi', Abu al-'Aliya and al-Suddi on the guidance, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passages 2:212 and 2:213: al-Tabari's commentary closes 2:212 (vol. 4 pp. 274-275: the reports on those above the mockers, and "without reckoning") and begins 2:213 (pp. 275-279: the meaning of "umma" and who was "one community" per Ibn 'Abbas, Qatada, Mujahid, Ubayy ibn Ka'b, Ibn Zayd and al-Suddi, with his choice), source registered on 2:213, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passages 2:210-2:212: al-Tabari's commentary closes 2:210 (vol. 4 pp. 266-270: the long report on the standing place with the editor's grading of its chain and Ibn Kathir's remarks, his argument for the raised reading "and the angels", and "the matter is decided"), covers 2:211 (pp. 270-273: the signs given to the Children of Israel and the changing of God's favour) and begins 2:212 (p. 273), with the source registered on 2:211 and 2:212, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:210: al-Tabari's commentary continues (vol. 4 pp. 261-266): the readings of "the angels" and "zulal/zilal" with his choices, whether the cloud belongs to God's coming or the angels', the grading note on the arches-in-the-cloud report, and the views on how God's coming is understood (no description beyond His own words; movement; His command; His reward and reckoning), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passages 2:208-2:210: al-Tabari's commentary closes 2:208 (vol. 4 pp. 254-258: who is addressed by "enter into al-silm entirely", 'Ikrima's report on the Jewish converts and the Sabbath, "kaffa", and the footsteps of Satan), covers 2:209 (pp. 259-260: the slip after the clear proofs) and begins 2:210 (p. 260), with the source registered on 2:209 and 2:210, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passages 2:207 and 2:208: al-Tabari's commentary closes 2:207 (vol. 4 pp. 248-251: the reports of Hisham ibn 'Amir, the man of Bajila and others, his preference for the one who commands the right, and "God is kind to His servants") and begins 2:208 (pp. 251-254: the meaning of "silm", the fatha and kasra readings and his choice, with the editor's notes), source registered on 2:208, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passages 2:205-2:207: al-Tabari's commentary closes 2:205 (vol. 4 pp. 243-244, the verbal noun of "fasad"), begins 2:206 (pp. 244-246: "pride seizes him in sin", the reports of 'Ali and of 'Umar with Ibn 'Abbas, "wretched is the resting place") and begins 2:207 (pp. 246-248: the sale of the self, the grammatical discussion, and the reports on Suhayb, Abu Dharr and the Meccan emigrant), with the source registered on 2:206 and 2:207, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:205 gains more of al-Tabari's commentary (vol. 4 pp. 238-243): the views on "ifsad" and his reasoning, the destruction of crops and offspring (al-Suddi, Mujahid, Ibn 'Abbas, Qatada, al-Dahhak, al-Rabi', 'Ata', Makhul), his rejection of the raised reading "yuhliku" citing Ubayy's codex, and "God does not love corruption", in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passages 2:204 and 2:205: al-Tabari's commentary continues in 2:204 (vol. 4 pp. 234-237: the two readings of "yushhidu/yashhadu" and his choice, and the views on "aladd al-khisam") and begins in 2:205 (pp. 237-238: "tawalla" and "sa'a", with the source registered), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:204 gains al-Tabari's commentary from volume 4 (pp. 229-233), with the source registered on its data file: the verse on the one whose speech pleases and who calls God to witness, and the reports on whom it concerns (al-Akhnas ibn Shariq per al-Suddi; the hypocrites who spoke about the Raji' expedition per Ibn 'Abbas; all hypocrites per Muhammad ibn Ka'b, Nawf, Qatada, Mujahid, al-Rabi' and 'Ata'), and the reading "yushhidu" with Ibn Zayd's report, with the editor's notes, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:203 closes its al-Tabari commentary from volume 4 (pp. 224-229): the rest of his argument against the "no hardship" and "until next year" readings (labelled his), the hadith of 'A'isha on what becomes lawful after throwing, sacrificing and shaving, the grammar of the lam in "for whoever fears God", and "fear God and know that you will be gathered to Him", with the editor's notes on the chains, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:203 gains al-Tabari's commentary from volume 4 (pp. 219-224): the remaining views on "no sin on him" (until the next year's hajj; if he keeps God-fearing; if he avoids game; forgiven), his own choice that it means the sins are removed for whoever fears God in his hajj (labelled his), the hadiths on following hajj with 'umra and returning as on the day one's mother bore one (Ibn Mas'ud, 'Umar, Ibn 'Abbas), and the start of his argument against the "no hardship" reading, with the editor's notes, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:203 gains al-Tabari's commentary from volume 4 (pp. 215-219): the reports on "whoever hastens in two days, there is no sin on him" taken as no sin in hastening or delaying (Mujahid, al-Suddi, Qatada, Ibrahim, Ibn 'Umar, Ibn 'Abbas, 'Ata'), and as forgiveness for both (Ibn Mas'ud, Ibn 'Umar, Ibn 'Abbas, 'Ali, al-Sha'bi, Mu'awiya ibn Qurra), with the editor's notes, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:203 gains al-Tabari's commentary from volume 4 (pp. 210-215): the rest of the reports on the "counted days" (days of tashriq), the hadiths that they are days of eating, drinking and remembrance of God (Abu Hurayra, 'A'isha, 'Amr ibn Dinar, al-Zuhri, 'Ali), his argument distinguishing the counted days from the known days of Hajj 22:28 (labelled his), and the start of "whoever hastens in two days" (the reports of 'Ata', al-Hasan, 'Ikrima), with the editor's notes on narrators, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passages 2:202 and 2:203 gain al-Tabari's commentary from volume 4 (pp. 206-210), with the source registered on both data files: 2:202 ("those will have a share of what they earned" and "swift in reckoning") and the start of 2:203 ("remember God in counted days" as the days of tashriq, with the reports of Ibn 'Abbas, 'Ata', Mujahid, Qatada, al-Hasan and Malik), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:200 ends its al-Tabari commentary from volume 4 (pp. 202-203; the reports of Anas, Mujahid, Qatada, al-Suddi and Ibn Zayd's three kinds of people at the rites), and passage 2:201 begins with it (pp. 203-206), with the source registered on its data file: the meaning of "good in this world and good in the hereafter" (well-being; knowledge and worship; wealth), the hadiths of Qatada and Anas on the sick man who asked to be punished early, and his own choice that "good" is general (labelled his), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:200 gains al-Tabari's commentary from volume 4 (pp. 196-201): the rest of the reports on remembering God as one remembers one's fathers (the boasting of the Jahiliyya at Mina; the child calling its father), his own choice that the remembrance is obedience and possibly the takbir of the days of Mina (labelled his), and the start of "among the people is he who says, Our Lord, give us in this world", in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:199 gains al-Tabari's commentary from volume 4 (pp. 183-195), and passage 2:200 begins with it (pp. 195-196), with the source registered on both data files: the commentary on "then pour forth from where the people pour forth" (the Hums of Quraysh, the view that "the people" means Ibrahim, and his own choice) that had been filed under 2:198 by mistake is moved to 2:199, with the new reports on "ask God's forgiveness" (al-'Abbas ibn Mirdas and Ibn 'Umar hadiths on forgiveness of claims at Jam', with the editor's grading notes), and 2:200 opens with "when you have completed your rites" and the reports on boasting of fathers, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:198 gains al-Tabari's commentary from volume 4 (pp. 186-191): the rest of the reports on "then pour forth from where the people pour forth" (the Hums of Quraysh: Mujahid, Qatada, al-Suddi, al-Rabi', Ibn Abi Najih's long account, 'A'isha), the view that "the people" means Ibrahim (al-Dahhak), and his own choice that Quraysh and their Hums allies are addressed (labelled his) with his argument and the note on pluralised singulars, with the editor's notes, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:198 gains al-Tabari's commentary from volume 4 (pp. 181-186): his explanation of Ibn 'Umar's and 'Abd al-Rahman ibn al-Aswad's remarks on the mash'ar (labelled his), "remember Him as He guided you" with the two grammatical analyses of "in", and the start of "then pour forth from where the people pour forth" with the Hums/Quraysh reports ('A'isha, 'Urwa, Ibn 'Abbas, 'Ata'), with the editor's notes, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:198 gains al-Tabari's commentary from volume 4 (pp. 176-181): the reports on the extent of the mash'ar (Ibn 'Umar, Mujahid, 'Ata', Ibn 'Abbas, al-Suddi, Sa'id ibn Jubayr, Ibn al-Zubayr), his limit of it at Muhassir and his own choice that the pilgrim stand at Quzah (labelled his) with the reports of 'Ali and Abu Bakr, with the editor's notes on chains, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:198 gains al-Tabari's commentary from volume 4 (pp. 171-176): the last grammarians' view on the name 'Arafat, the reports on why it is so called (al-Suddi's account of Ibrahim, Nu'aym ibn Abi Hind, 'Ali via Ibn al-Musayyib, Ibn 'Abbas, 'Ata'), his own view on the form of the name (labelled his), and the start of "remember God at the Sacred Landmark" with his definition of the mash'ar and the reports of Ibn 'Umar, Ibn 'Abbas and Sa'id ibn Jubayr, with the editor's notes, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:198 gains al-Tabari's commentary from volume 4 (pp. 166-171): the rest of the reports on trade in the hajj season (Qatada, al-Rabi', Ibn al-Zubayr, Ibn 'Abbas, Sa'id ibn Jubayr, Mujahid, 'Umar, Ibn 'Umar), and the start of "when you pour forth from 'Arafat" with the meaning of "ifada" and the Basran and Kufan grammarians' views on the form of the name 'Arafat, with the editor's notes, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:198 gains al-Tabari's commentary from volume 4 (pp. 162-166), with the source registered on its data file: "no blame on you in seeking bounty" (trade during ihram and the hajj season), the reports of Ibn 'Abbas, Mujahid, Buraida and Ibn 'Umar (the camel-hirers' question) and the reading attributed to Ibn 'Abbas with added words, plus the editor's notes, in Arabic, English and Urdu.
- Passage 2:197 gains al-Tabari's commentary from volume 4 (pp. 158-162): the rest of the reports on "take provision" (people of Yemen, Bedouin and tribes who travelled without provisions; what counted as provision: sawiq, ka'k, dates), his summary of the whole verse, and his comment on "fear Me, O people of understanding", with the editor's notes, in Arabic, English and Urdu. This closes 2:197.
- Passage 2:197 gains al-Tabari's commentary from volume 4 (pp. 153-158): the close of his argument on jidal, his reading preference (rafath and fusuq nominative, jidal with fatha; the Basrans, Ibn Kathir, Abu 'Amr), his dismissal of the weaker jidal views (labelled his), the comments on "whatever good you do, God knows it", and the start of "take provision" with the reports on pilgrims who travelled without provisions (Ibn 'Umar, Ibn 'Abbas, 'Ikrima, Ibrahim, Mujahid, Sa'id ibn Jubayr, al-Sha'bi, Salim), in Arabic, English and Urdu.
